L716: Note that out of 210 patients in pivotal Study BLC2001 (all three dosing regimens), only 3 patients had a concomitant strong CYP2C9 and none had a concomitant strong CYP3A inhibitor. No apparent safety difference was observed between patients with or without CYP2C9 / CYP3A inhibitors (including weak, moderate, strong inhibitors)—although there were slightly higher rates of adverse events leading to dose interruption, reductions, treatment discontinuations in patients with concomitant CYP2C9 / CYP3A inhibitors.
L717: ## **Effect of rifampin (strong CYP3A/moderate CYP3A inducer) on erdafitinib PK**
L718: The Applicant used PBPK modeling to evaluate the effect of concomitant rifampin on the exposure of erdafitinib. Co-administration of rifampin (600 mg QD for 15 d) and erdafitinib (9 mg QD) was predicted to decrease erdafitinib steady-state AUC by 62% and Cmax by 51% (Table 62). Refer to the PBPK review in the Appendix for details.
L719: **Table 62: Predicted Effect of Concomitant Rifampin on Erdafitinib Exposure**
L720: Strong CYP3A/Moderate CYP3A Inducer	Erdafitinib Dosing	Erdafitinib Geometric Mean Ratio (+rifampin/-rifampin)
AUC	Cmax
600 mg rifampin QD	9 mg QD (steady-state)	0.38	0.49
L721: Source: Adapted from Tables 33 in Applicant's AG120-C-001-PBPK Report
L722: Although the PBPK modeling was found to be adequate in predicting the effect of rifampin of erdafitinib PK, the inherent variability of prediction tools should be considered. In this case, it is important to have clinical DDI data to pinpoint the magnitude change in erdafitinib exposure with concomitant strong CYP2C9 / CYP3A inducers to match exposure, given the possible loss of efficacy based on exposure-response for efficacy. Therefore, based on inherent variability and uncertainty regarding the predicted decrease in erdafitinib steady-state AUC with rifampin,
L723: concomitant strong CYP3A inducers should be avoided. A PMR will be issued for the Applicant to conduct a clinical DDI study to evaluate the effect of a strong CYP2C9 / CYP3A inducer (e.g., rifampin) on erdafitinib exposure.
L724: Co-administration of BALVERSA with moderate inducers of CYP2C9 or CYP3A4 may decrease erdafitinib plasma concentrations. Decreased erdafitinib plasma concentrations may lead to decreased activity. If a moderate CYP2C9 or CYP3A4 inducer must be co-administered at the start of erdafitinib treatment, administer BALVERSA dose as recommended (8 mg once daily with potential to increase to 9 mg once daily based on serum phosphate levels on Days 14 to 21 and tolerability). If a moderate CYP2C9 or CYP3A4 inducer must be co-administered after the initial dose increase period based on serum phosphate levels and tolerability, increase BALVERSA dose up to 9 mg. When a moderate inducer of CYP2C9 or CYP3A4 is discontinued, continue BALVERSA at the same dose, in the absence of drug-related toxicity.
L725: This recommendation is based on anticipated decrease in erdafitinib exposure with concomitant moderate or weak CYP2C9 / CYP3A inducers, with recommendation to increase dose up to 9 mg based on tolerability. This recommendation will be revisited after the results of PMC study with a strong inducer of CYP2C9 and CYP3A4 is available.
L726: ## Effect of erdafitinib on a P-gp substrate PK
L727: The Applicant used PBPK modeling to evaluate the effect of erdafitinib on the PK of concomitant digoxin (P-gp substrate), however the modeling was considered qualitative, and not quantitative, due to uncertainty in translating in vitro K_i to in vivo K_i. Based on the qualitative analysis using an absorption model simulation (refer to PBPK review in the Appendix for details), erdafitinib dose should be separated by \ge 6 h before or after administration of P-gp substrates with narrow therapeutic index.
L728: ## *Drug-Drug Interaction (in vitro)*
L729: ## Effect of erdafitinib on a CYP3A substrate PK
L730: Based on *in vitro* studies, erdafitinib is a time-dependent inhibitor of CYP3A and an inducer of CYP3A, however the results were inconclusive to rule out a net inhibition or induction effect of erdafitinib on CYP3A substrates *in vivo*. The Applicant used PBPK modeling to evaluate the effect of erdafitinib on the PK of concomitant midazolam (sensitive CYP3A substrate), however the modeling was found to be inadequate to support Applicant's proposed labeling that PBPK predicted a lack of clinically relevant DDI between erdafitinib and CYP3A substrates (refer to PBPK review in the Appendix for details). Therefore, concomitant CYP3A substrates with narrow therapeutic index should be avoided. A PMR will be issued for the Applicant to conduct a clinical DDI study to evaluate the effect of erdafitinib on the exposure of a CYP3A sensitive substrate.
L731: ## Effect of erdafitinib on an OCT2 substrate PK
L732: Based on in vitro study, an in vivo interaction between erdafitinib and a OCT2 substrate cannot be ruled out (C_{max,u}/IC_{50} = 0.33 but increases to 1.0 when f_{u,p} is capped at 1%, per FDA Draft Guidance for DDI, for drugs that are &gt;99% bound to human plasma proteins). The Applicant used PBPK modeling to evaluate the effect of erdafitinib on the PK of concomitant metformin (OCT2 substrate), however the metformin model found to be inadequate to support Applicant's proposed labeling that PBPK predicted a lack of clinically relevant DDI between erdafitinib and metformin (refer to PBPK review in the Appendix for details). Therefore, for concomitant OCT2 substrates, alternative agents should be considered or the dose of OCT2 substrates should be reduced as tolerated. A PMR will be issued for the Applicant to conduct a clinical DDI study to evaluate the effect of erdafitinib on the exposure of a probe OCT2 substrate.
L733: ## Effect of gastric pH modulators on erdafitinib PK
L734: Erdafitinib has adequate solubility across the pH range of 1 to 7.4. Acid-lowering agents (e.g., antacids, H2-antagonists, proton pump inhibitors) are not expected to affect the bioavailability of erdafitinib. Therefore, no dose adjustment is needed in patients taking concomitant gastric pH modulators.
L735: ## Effect of phosphate level-altering agents on patient serum phosphate levels
L736: In Applicant's pivotal Study BLC2001, the use of drugs which can increase serum phosphate levels, such as potassium phosphate supplements, vitamin D supplements, antacids, phosphate-containing enemas or laxatives, and medications known to have phosphate as an excipient (unless no alternatives exist) were prohibited. To manage hyperphosphatemia, phosphate binders were permitted. To ensure that the serum phosphate level signal for dose increase is attributed to erdafitinib only, agents that can alter serum phosphate levels before the initial dose increase period based on serum phosphate level (Days 14-21) should be avoided.

L747: ## 7 Sources of Clinical Data and Review Strategy
L748: ## 7.1. Table of Clinical Studies
L749: The Applicant has submitted three clinical studies to support NDA 212018 (Study BLC2001, Study EDI1001, and Study GAC1001). This NDA is primarily based on findings from 87 patients with relapsed/refractory advanced or metastatic urothelial cancer and susceptible FGFR genomic alterations enrolled in Cohort 3 of study BLC2001. These patients all received erdafitinib at the proposed dose of 8 mg, with PD-guided up-titration to 9 mg in patients. Our efficacy review primarily focused on data from these 87 patients who received the dose under review in the proposed indication population.

L758: ## 7.2. Review Strategy
L759: The clinical and statistical review included the following:
L760: - Review of the current literature on urothelial carcinoma epidemiology, and treatment, including preclinical and clinical data on therapies targeting the FGFR pathway and of the natural history of FGFR-altered urothelial cancer
- Review of Applicant-submitted clinical study BLC2001, including CSR, protocols, protocol amendments, and SDTM and ADaM datasets
- Review of Applicant-submitted real-world data from the Flatiron-FMI Database, including white papers, protocols, and datasets
- Review of Applicant-submitted real-world data from the BRIDGE Consortium study including white papers, protocols, and datasets
- Review and assessment of Applicant analysis of erdafitinib efficacy and safety for evaluation of Applicant's claims
- Review of datasets and SAS programming algorithm submitted by the Applicant
- Use of the datasets to determine the baseline patient characteristics, verify reported response rate, and adverse event profile.
- Review of patient narratives of serious adverse events, deaths, and immune-mediated AEs
- Review of meeting minutes conducted during drug development
- Assessment of the Module 2 summaries, including the Summary of Clinical Efficacy and Summary of Clinical Safety
- Evaluation of reviews conducted by other FDA disciplines
- Review of consultation reports from the Office of Scientific Investigations
- Requests for additional information from the Applicant and review of Applicant responses
- Formulation of the benefit-risk analysis and recommendations
- Review and evaluation of proposed labeling
- EDR link for the electronic data sources is:
\\CDSesub1\evsprod\NDA212028\212018.enx
L761: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical and Evaluation
L762: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L763: ## 8.1.1. Study 42756493BLC2001 (BLC2001)
L764: ## Trial Design
L765: BLC2001 was a multi-center, open-label, Phase 2 clinical study of erdafitinib in patients with metastatic or surgically unresectable urothelial cancer harboring select FGFR genetic alterations.
L766: ## Key Eligibility Criteria
L767: - Metastatic or surgically unresectable urothelial cancer (cT4b, N+, M+). A minor component of patients (&lt;50% overall) with variant histologies (glandular or squamous differentiation, or evolution to more aggressive phenotypes such as sarcomatoid or micropapillary change) were eligible for enrolment
- Disease progression following:Chemotherapy for metastatic disease
- Progression within 12 months of receiving chemotherapy in the neoadjuvant or adjuvant setting
- Patients who were ineligible for cisplatin-based chemotherapy were also eligible for enrolment. Platinum ineligibility was based upon:Impaired renal function defined as a glomerular filtration rate (GFR) &lt;60 mL/min/1.73 m2 by 24-hour urine measurement or calculated by Cockcroft-Gault
- Grade 2 or higher peripheral neuropathy by CTCAE version 4.0
- All patients must have had FGFR mutation or fusion:FGFR3 gene mutations: R248C, S249C, G370C, and Y373C,
- FGFR fusions: FGFR2-BICC1, FGFR2-CASP7, FGFR3-TACC3, and FGFR3-BAIAP2L1.
- ECOG performance status score 0, 1, or 2
- Adequate bone marrow, liver, and renal function
- Absence or persistent phosphate level &gt; upper limit of normal during screening
L768: **Reviewer Comments:** Although cisplatin-ineligible patients were enrolled on BLC2001, they were not ultimately included in the indication population for this approval due to insufficient numbers enrolled in the indicated dosing regimen (Regimen 3, see section below on Study Treatment) to adequately assess the efficacy of erdafitinib in these patients (n=12).
L769: ## Study Treatment
L770: There were 3 dosing regimens evaluated in patients on BLC2001;
L771: - Regimen 1: 10 mg once daily, 7 days on/ 7 days off
- Regimen 2: 6 mg once daily, every day
L772: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation (NDA [NME] 212018)
## BALVERSA™ (erdafitinib)
L773: - Regimen 3: 8 mg once daily, with pharmacologic-guided dose increase to 9 mg based on tolerance and serum phosphate levels
L774: At the initiation of the study, patients were randomized to Regimen 1 or Regimen 2. At Interim Analysis 1 (IA1), the Data Review Committee (DRC) recommended the selection of Regimen 2 for continued development and also recommended additional safety and efficacy analyses. Based on these additional analyses and PK/PD modeling, the DRC recommended that BLC2001 use Regimen 3 for continued development. These changes were implemented in Amendment 3 (August 9, 2016).
L775: **Figure 5: Study Schema (BLC2001)**
L776: *Source: Clinical Study Report for 42756493BLC2001*
L777: ## Dose Increase Schema
L778: For patients treated with Regimen 3, erdafitinib dosing was increased or maintained based on phosphate level measured on Cycle 1 Day 14, with observed toxicity to that point also considered
L779: Figure 6). The increased dose was given starting on Cycle 1 Day 15.
L780: Figure 6: Dose increase based on phosphate levels, Regimen 3 (BLC2001)
L781: * PO4 assessments are done as per T&amp;E; dose up-titration decision is based on C1D14 assessment and up-titrated dose to be administered starting C1D15
L782: ** Drug related toxicity during Cycle 1 as determined by investigator. At any time during treatment with study drug, dose interruptions/modifications would be managed by investigator as clinically indicated; specific recommendations on dose reductions and toxicity management are provided throughout Section 6.)
L783: Source: Clinical Study Report for 42756493BLC2001
L784: ## Study Monitoring
L785: The study was conducted in an outpatient setting. Subjects were seen at the study center on pre-specified days for safety monitoring, pharmacokinetic and biomarker sample collection, and provision of clinical supplies. Safety was monitored routinely throughout the study by the sponsor's study responsible physician and scientist as well as the sponsor's safety management team. Safety review was based on reported adverse events, clinical laboratory tests, vital sign measurements, physical examinations, ophthalmologic examinations, and electrocardiogram (ECG) findings. In addition, plasma concentrations were measured for pharmacokinetic and pharmacokinetic/pharmacodynamic evaluations. Subjects were assessed for disease response every 6 weeks during the first 3 months. Thereafter, scans were performed every 12 weeks for the next 9 months, then every 4 to 6 months until disease progression.
L786: ## Radiology Charter
L787: Radiographic scans for patients enrolled to Regimen 3 were independently reviewed by PAREXEL Informatics, Medical Imaging and Patient Technology Solutions. Each imaging timepoint for a patient was assessed by one (single read) independent radiologist who determined an overall tumor assessment at each post-baseline timepoint according to RECIST (version 1.1). After the timepoint by timepoint radiology review, the same independent radiologist(s) globally assessed the reviewed timepoints and confirmed or updated their previous overall tumor assessments.
L788: ## Study Endpoints
L789: The primary study endpoint was the confirmed best objective response (BOR), defined as the best (overall) objective response a patient achieved during the study in the order of Complete Response (CR), Partial Response (PR), Stable Disease (SD), Progressive Disease (PD), where CR and PR are confirmed as per RECIST 1.1. Patients without measurable disease at baseline or without post-baseline tumor assessment are considered non-responders. Responders are patients with best response of CR or PR.
L790: Response was evaluated using RECIST version 1.1. Response was assessed by investigators for all patients in the study as well as by a Blinded Independent Review Committee (BIRC) for patients in the 8-mg daily regimen (Regimen 3). The confirmed objective response rate (ORR) is the proportion of patients with a confirmed BOR of CR or PR. Per protocol, if the lower bound of 95% 2-sided confidence interval for the response rate assessed by the investigator was above 25%, the response assessed by BIRC was to be used for the primary and final analyses, and the response assessed by the investigators was to be used for supportive analyses.
L791: The Applicant submitted an Independent Review Charter with [REDACTED] (b) (4)
[REDACTED] Refer to subsection “Radiology Charter” for additional details.
L792: Secondary efficacy endpoints included the following:
L793: - Duration of Response (DOR), calculated from the date of initial documentation of a response (CR or PR) to the date of first documented evidence of progressive disease (or relapse for patients who experience CR during the study) or death.
- Progression-free survival (PFS), defined as the duration from the date of the first dose of study drug until the date of first documented evidence of progressive disease (or relapse for patients who experience CR during the study) or death, whichever comes first.
- Overall Survival (OS), measured from the date of first dose of study drug to the date of patient's death.
L794: **Reviewer Comments:** In single arm trials, FDA does not use inferential procedures to evaluate trial results. Instead, the efficacy evaluation is based on the magnitude of response rate and adequate duration of response. Additionally, we note that time-to-event endpoints are uninterpretable without a comparator arm.
L795: ## Patient reported outcome (PRO) endpoints
L796: PROs were assessed using the Symptom Measurement Questionnaire (SMQ). SMQ is a self-administered PRO instrument that measures symptoms experienced by patients with urothelial
L797: cancer. It consists of five items covering five different symptoms including pain, fatigue, cough, shortness of breath, and blood in urine. The change from baseline to each time point for the overall symptom score and each item were to be summarized.
L798: ## Analysis Population
L799: The Applicant defined the primary analysis population as all patients who were treated with at least 1 dose of study drug in Regimen 3 (n=99).
L800: **Reviewer Comments:** The Applicant and FDA agreed that the patients with chemo-relapsed/refractory disease would be the primary efficacy population in Regimen 3 (n=87) and would form the basis for the labeled indication population. Patients ineligible for cisplatin-based therapy (n=12) were excluded from the FDA primary efficacy analysis.
L801: In addition, FDA review of efficacy included evaluating patients based on type of FGFR genetic alteration (point mutation versus gene fusion; FGFR2 versus FGFR3). The FGFR2 test kit was not validated by FDA. The following 6 patients were detected with FGFR2 gene fusions in the primary analysis population of 87 patients.
L802: (b) (6)
L803: FGFR2: BICC1 –
FGFR2: CASP7 –
L804: Of the 6 patients, patient (b) (6) also had a detected fusion involving FGFR3, and was positive for fusions of both FGFR2: CASP7 and FGFR3-TACC3-V3.
L805: ## Subgroup Analyses
L806: The Applicant submitted pre-specified subgroup analyses:
L807: - Response by FGFR Alterations
- Response by Prior Lines of Therapy
- Response by Tumor Location
- Response by Demographic and Baseline Disease Characteristics
L808: **Reviewer Comments:** Additional subgroup analyses were performed during the FDA analysis of this NDA:
L809: - FGFR genetic alteration
- FGFR genetic alteration subtype (point mutation versus gene fusion)
- FGFR genetic alteration location (FGFR2 versus FGFR3)
- Risk prognostic scoring systems:MSKCC score
- Bellmunt score
- Presence or absence of liver metastases
L810: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L811: The primary hypothesis was that the selected dose regimen had an objective response rate &gt; 25% in patients with metastatic or surgically unresectable urothelial cancer harboring select FGFR alterations.
L812: Sample Size Consideration: Regimen 3 planned to enroll approximately 100 patients, with at least 80 chemo-relapsed/refractory patients. If the true response rate is 42%, 88 patients would provide at least 85% power to obtain the lower bound of 95% 2-sided confidence interval above 25%.
L813: **Reviewer Comments:** The CSR submitted for this NDA was based on the FGFR-positive patients with the number of treated chemo-relapsed/refractory patients consisting of N=87 patients and a database cutoff of March 15, 2018.
L814: For the analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint of ORR per RECIST 1.1 as assessed by BIRC, the ORR was summarized by the percentage of confirmed responses (CR or PR), and its 95% 2-sided confidence interval (CI) were calculated using normal approximation to the binomial distribution. The secondary endpoints of DOR, PFS, and OS were analyzed using Kaplan-Meier methods. DOR was only assessed in responders.
L815: ## Protocol Amendments
L816: The study protocol was proposed on January 19, 2015. Before its initiation on March 25, 2015 and during the study, the protocol was amended with feedback from the Agency. There were 5 amendments, the third of which added Regimen 3.

L821: The original statistical analysis plan (SAP) was approved on 04 June 2015. The SAP amendment 1 (based on Protocol Amendment 3, dated 09 August 2016) was finalized on 29 September 2016. Dosing Regimen 3 (8 mg once daily (with provision for up-titration to 9 mg once daily) was added following the recommendation of the DRC at Interim Analysis 1. The primary efficacy analysis population was changed to the patients treated at Regimen 3 in the SAP amendment 1. Additionally, the original planned Interim Analysis 2 for Regimen 1 and 2 were removed, and analysis of Symptom Measurement Questionnaire was added.
L822: **Reviewer Comment:** Overall, the protocol amendments do not appear to have affected the integrity of the study. Additionally, we note that the SAP considered all enrolled patients as the primary efficacy population, including cisplatin-ineligible patients. SAP amendment 1 was finalized at the time of adding regimen 3. In Amendment 1, the chemo-relapsed/refractory population was considered as a secondary objective only.
L823: ## 8.1.2. Study Results
L824: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L825: The Applicant attests that clinical study 42756493BLC2001 was conducted in compliance with
L826: Good Clinical Practice, including the archival of essential Documents. It was conducted in accordance with the ethical principles that have their origin in the Declaration of Helsinki and that are consistent with Good Clinical Practices and applicable regulatory requirements. Known instances of nonconformance were documented and are not considered to have had an impact on the overall conclusions of this study.
L827: DOP1 consulted the OSI on 10/24/2018 to perform an audit of select clinical sites. Sites were selected based on the high number of patients enrolled, the high proportion of responders, and the low overall reporting of safety data such as adverse events and serious adverse events. Two domestic sites were chosen for inspection: Sites US00011 and US00014. One international site was chosen: Site FR00005 in France. Additionally, Janssen Biotech, Inc. was selected for inspection.
L828: Three clinical sites, Dr. Arlene Siefker Radtke (Site US00011), Dr. Scott Tagawa, (US00014), and Dr. Yohann Loriot (Site FR00005), were selected for audit. The inspections conducted by OSI identified the following items:
L829: - Dr. Arlene Siefker Radtke (Site US00011): The site screened 53 patients and enrolled 5 patients, including 4 in Regimen 3). The inspection revealed no objectionable observations in the conduct at this site.
- Dr. Scott Tagawa, (US00014). The site screened 31 patients and enrolled 6 patients, including 5 in Regimen 3. There was a verbal observation regarding out of window scans regarding subject [REDACTED] (b) (6). It was also noted that the subject was an inclusion/exclusion criteria failure reported in the background materials. SAEs were reviewed with no observations and the study had proper approval and communication with the IRB.
- Dr. Yohann Loriot (Site FR00005): The site screened 104 patients and enrolled 10 patients, including 5 in Regimen 3. The inspection revealed no significant deficiencies. For one subject ([REDACTED] (b) (6) on Regimen 2) who had a partial response at the first on-study assessment, there was a difference in reporting of the second assessment between the data listing (progressive disease as assessed by Investigator) and the source radiology report (stable disease). The Investigator explained that based on his review with another radiologist and to his assessment, the second assessment showed disease progression, consistent with the reported data listing by the applicant.

L832: ## Patient Disposition
L833: Overall, 2214 patients with tissue samples were screened for molecular eligibility for enrollment, of which 417 patients had tissue specimens that were adequate for molecular screening and that were found to carry an FGFR genetic alteration. Of the 417 patients, 224
L834: patients met the molecular eligibility criteria (FGFR+) but did not meet the other study eligibility criteria. Twenty of these FGFR+ patients were treated with erdafitinib despite not meeting eligibility criteria that would have otherwise made them screen failures. Of these patients, 6 had received prior chemotherapy and received Regimen 3 (Table 65).

L842: ## **Reviewer Comments:**
L843: Review of the results of patient screening process for BLC2001 identified 6 patients enrolled and treated with Regimen 3, but who did not meet all eligibility criteria. These events were recorded as major protocol deviations. There was one partial response achieved by these 6 patients. These deviations do not have a significant impact on the efficacy results.
L844: ## **Protocol Violations/Deviations**
L845: Major protocol deviations were categorized in the SAP for Study BLC2001 (Section 4.4) as the following:
L846: - Patient safety
- Eligibility criteria
- Efficacy assessment deviation
- Treatment compliance
L847: Sixteen of the 87 patients (18%) in Regimen 3 who received prior chemotherapy experienced 25 protocol violations. The most frequent type of protocol deviation is related to safety assessments (13 deviations) that were not performed within the allowed window of time provided by the protocol. Eligibility Criteria Unmet was the second most frequent type of protocol deviation.

L868: **Reviewer's comment:** In these molecularly-defined patients, reported response rate to prior platinum-based chemotherapy was approximately 35%, which is in line with expected response rates. This is in contrast to the 1 patient of 21 who responded to treatment with prior immunotherapy, which is an especially low response rate compared to expected responses to immunotherapy (see Table 1). The review team took this submitted information into account when considering whether patients whose tumors harbor FGFR genetic alterations represent a subset of urothelial cancer to which the OS advantage demonstrated by pembrolizumab in the second-line setting might not apply, as these patients may be expected to do less well on immunotherapy.
L869: Table 71 provides information on the extent of disease at baseline in the enrolled patients. Two
L870: scoring systems have been used in the literature to assess prognostic factors in patients with urothelial cancer, the Bellmunt and MSKCC scoring systems. These have been used in urothelial cancer and have prognostic value in determination of OS. The Bellmunt scoring system uses performance status, hemoglobin, and the presence of liver metastases. The MSKCC system uses performance status and the presence of visceral (bone, liver, and lung) metastases.

L935: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints
L936: The Applicant collected patient reported outcome (PRO) using the Symptom Measurement Questionnaire (SMQ). The SMQ was a self-administered instrument measuring symptoms experienced by patients with urothelial cancer. It consists of five items covering five different symptoms including pain, fatigue, cough, shortness of breath, and blood in urine. Responses to each of the five items are from 1 to 7 on a Likert scale, with higher scores indicating a more severe degree of symptomatology. An overall symptom score is generated by summing the score of the five items and dividing the total by five. If there were missing items, the total score was calculated by the sum of all completed items and dividing it by the number of completed items. A higher score indicates higher degree of symptoms severity.
L937: The Global Impression of Change (Symptoms) is a single-item self-administered PRO to rate the change in health since the start of the trial. A 7-point response scale is used, with a lower score indicating greater improvement in patient's self-reported health.
L938: The Global Impression of Change (Quality of Life) is a single-item self-administered PRO to rate the change in health-related quality of life (HRQoL) since the start of the trial. A 7-point response scale is used, with a lower score indicating greater improvement in patient's self-reported HRQoL.
L939: The questionnaire was implemented with Amendment 3 to the protocol. The questionnaire was administered only to patients in the 8-mg regimen who could read English. The questionnaire time recall period for this instrument was 1 week (the past week) and was completed by the patient at baseline, Cycle 1 Day 14, Cycle 2 Day 1, Cycle 3 Day 1, and at the end of treatment.
L940: Shifts from baseline to worst values post-baseline were analyzed for each specific symptom, including pain, fatigue, cough, shortness of breath, and blood in the urine. Only 22 of the 87 patients had values at both baseline and post-baseline and were included in the analyses. Worsening in scores from \le 3 at baseline to &gt;3 during treatment were most common for fatigue, reported for 8 patients (36%). Other shifts from \le 3 at baseline to &gt;3 during treatment were as
L941: follows: 3 patients for shortness of breath (14%), 2 patients (9.1%) each for pain and cough, and 1 patient (4.5%) for blood in the urine.
L942: **Reviewer Comments:** The SMQ is a composite that incorporates self-reported ability to function, pain, and general health status as filled out by patients. This instrument is a generic preference-based measure intended to determine if response to therapy and side effects of therapy are accompanied by measurable changes in symptoms reported. This questionnaire is limited in its ability to ascertain the cause of any decreased level of functioning or quality of life for an individual patient. Moreover, there were only 22 patients who had values at both baseline and post-baseline. Due to the small number and limitations, no conclusion can be made based on PRO data generated from the SMQ.
L943: ## 8.1.3. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials (including Real-World Evidence)
L944: As discussed with the FDA in the pre-NDA meeting of July 2018, the Applicant conducted a Real-World Evidence (RWE) retrospective study using the Flatiron-FMI and the Bladder Cancer Research Initiative for Drug Targeting in Germany (BRIDGE) databases to provide natural history and prognostic information on patients with metastatic urothelial cancers with FGFR alterations. This study was performed with the intention of demonstrating that patients with FGFR alterations have lower rates of response to anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy including pembrolizumab, which has regular approval for the treatment of platinum-refractory advanced urothelial cancer.
L945: ## Flatiron-FMI Database
L946: ## Study Design
L947: Flatiron Health (Flatiron) collected real-world, longitudinal, de-identified patient-level clinical data from electronic health records (EHRs) from US community-based cancer clinics. In partnership with Foundation Medicine, Inc. (FMI), Flatiron patient EHRs were linked with FoundationOne next-generation sequencing (NGS) results to generate a database termed the Flatiron-FMI clinico-genomic (CG) database. The Flatiron-FMI CG database thus included patients with both a confirmed diagnosis of advanced bladder cancer and with FoundationOne testing results for FGFR status available.
L948: ## FGFR Population Definitions
L949: The primary analysis population focused on FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative patients as per the specific mutations used in the Janssen diagnostic panel used for patient selection in the BLC2001 Phase 2 study of erdafitinib. In addition, a sensitivity analysis assessed all-FGFR-altered patients detected to have any FGFR alterations.
L950: The specific definitions of these populations include:
L951: - **Janssen-FGFR-positive**: patients positive for 1 or more of the 9 FGFR mutations and gene fusions in the Janssen diagnostic panel (FGFR3 gene mutation: S249C, Y373C, 248C, G370C; FGFR gene fusion: FGFR3-TACC3v1, FGFR3-TACC3v3, FGFR3-BAIAP2L1, FGFR2-CASP7, FGFR2-BICC1)
- **Janssen-FGFR-negative**: patients negative for 9 FGFR mutations and gene fusions in the Janssen diagnostic panel
- **All-FGFR-positive**: patients with all observed alterations in FGFR, including mutations, fusions, rearrangements, deletions and truncations
- **All-FGFR-negative**: patients for whom FGFR mutations, fusions, rearrangements, deletions, or truncations were not detected
L952: **Reviewer Comments:** To be consistent with Study BLC2001, the review team only focused on the FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative patients relative to the Janssen-FGFR diagnostic panel.
L953: ## Inclusion Criteria
L954: - Patient had at least 1 documented clinical visit in the Flatiron network on or after January 01, 2011
- Patient had an ICD code consistent with bladder cancer in their EHR
- Patient had a confirmed diagnosis of advanced bladder cancer (any histology)
- Patient had NGS testing by FMI on a tumor sample with pathologist-confirmed histology consistent with bladder cancer
- Patient had 1 of the following confirmed primary sites of disease: bladder, renal pelvis, ureter, or urethra
L955: ## Diagnosis Criteria
L956: Confirmation of these diagnoses were based upon review of Flatiron unstructured data and confirmation of histology from the patient's tumor sample received by FMI.
L957: ## Study Objectives
L958: **Part 1.** To assess the association between FGFR mutations or fusions and anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy outcome in locally advanced or metastatic bladder cancer, and to assess the prognostic value of FGFR alterations in the advanced disease setting
L959: **Part 2.** To compare OS among FGFR-positive patients treated with erdafitinib (from Study BLC2001) vs. those treated with currently available treatment options (i.e., anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy and/or chemotherapy) in the real-world setting (from Flatiron-FMI database)
L960: ## Analysis Sets
L961: ## **Part 1**
L962: - The treated patients with any (1st or 2nd) line of therapy analysis set included all patients who had any treatment with a 1st or 2nd line of therapy after the diagnosis date of
L963: advanced disease. This analysis was used to assess the prognostic value of FGFR mutations and gene fusions.
L964: - The treated patients with any (or 1st, or 2nd) line of anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy analysis set included all patients who had 1 of the 5 approved anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy (pembrolizumab, nivolumab, durvalumab, atezolizumab, or avelumab) used at any (or 1st, or 2nd) line of therapy after the advanced diagnosis date. This analysis set was used to assess the value of FGFR mutations and gene fusions for predicting anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy outcomes.
- The treated patients with any (or 1st, or 2nd) line of chemotherapy analysis set included all patients who had any chemotherapy at any (or 1st, or 2nd) line of therapy after the advanced diagnosis date. This analysis set was used to assess the value of FGFR mutations and gene fusions for predicting outcomes of chemotherapy treatment.
L965: ## Part 2
L966: - Patients from BLC2001 Regimen 3
FGFR-positive patients treated with the proposed registration dose regimen of erdafitinib 8 mg QD (Regimen 3) in BLC2001
- Patients in Flatiron-FMI databaseFGFR-positive patients treated with anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy
- FGFR-positive patients treated with chemotherapy
L967: ## Study Endpoints
L968: Overall survival (OS), defined as the index date to the date of death from any cause. The index date was the starting date of any or one specific treatment line.
L969: Real world tumor response (rwTR) was defined qualitatively as
L970: - Complete Response (CR): Complete resolution of all visible disease.
- Partial Response (PR): Partial reduction in size of visible disease in some or all areas without any areas of increase in visible disease.
- Stable Disease (SD): No change in overall size of visible disease.
- Progressive Disease (PD): Increase in visible disease and/or presence of any new lesions
- Pseudoprogression: Increase in tumor size, which the clinician states as possibly being an effect of immunotherapy.
- Indeterminate response: Situations in which the clinician explicitly states that he/she is not able to make a determination of the assessment.
- Not documented: There is a record of radiology scan but no record of a clinician assessment of the scan.
L971: **Reviewer Comments:** Real-world tumor response assessment is limited by the lack of utilization of a standardized approach (such as RECIST v.1.1) in the real-world setting to objectively assess and compare clinical response among patient cohorts. RECIST utilizes a numerically quantifiable change in pre-defined target lesions selected at baseline to determine clinical response status. In addition to sum of diameters, clinical protocols require confirmation of response, accounting for
L972: *response duration in RECIST response assignment. These quantifiable and time-dependent assessments are not typically captured in EHR data. Real-world tumor response is defined as a qualitative change in burden of disease over the course of treatment of a given therapy. The goal of rwTR data is to identify and document the point in time at which a patient's tumor shrinks or disappears. However, tumor response is assessed and described in a variety of ways in the real-world, and therefore the data usually carries large variability.*
L973: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L974: This is a retrospective study, and the sample size was not based on statistical consideration.
L975: ## Part 1
L976: Flatiron Health and FMI datasets were linked together and included patients who had at least 1 documented clinical visit in the Flatiron network on or after 01 January 2011, and those who underwent NGS testing by FMI on a tumor sample in the database. That meant patients who died before having FoundationOne testing, and/or who did not have a visit in the Flatiron network in or after 2011 were not included in the CG dataset. Thus, the dataset is subject to left truncation, which occurs when a dataset does not include patients with events that occur before a pre-specified time. To adjust for left truncation, a delayed entry model was used in a Cox proportional hazards model. Patients who received therapies under analyses prior to the FMI specimen report date were not considered as being in the risk set until the FMI specimen report date, i.e., their entry in the risk set was “delayed”. The entry date was the FMI specimen report date. The follow-up time for each patient extended from the entry date to the event date (or censor date).
L977: Multivariate analyses were performed using a selected set of potential prognostic variables (obtained at baseline, e.g., sex, age, tumor stage, tumor mutation burden, primary tumor location, transitional cell carcinoma, and smoking history) as covariates in Cox regression model for OS. Each factor was assessed individually in addition to the main factor of FGFR status in a bivariate model as well as including all covariates in a multivariate model to assess the impact of these factors on the effect of FGFR alterations.
L978: Real-world best tumor response rate (rwBTRR) and real-world disease control rate (rwDCR) were compared between FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative groups. Two-sided 95% confidence intervals (CIs) for the relative risks were calculated based on Wald chi-square statistics.
L979: **Reviewer Comments:** *The reviewer considers the delayed entry model reasonable in this analysis given the fact that the data from the Flatiron-FMI CG database was left truncated.*
L980: ## Part 2
L981: Propensity score weighting approaches were used in the analysis to compare OS between patients in BCL2001 Regimen 3 and FGFR-positive patients from the Flatiron-FMI database. Variables used for propensity score estimation were age, sex, line of treatment, smoking history, primary tumor location, hemoglobin level, and time since most recent therapy. The
L982: stabilized inverse probability treatment weighting (sIPTW) approach was used with propensity score estimate as the weight to assess treatment effect on OS in the proportional hazards regression model. Hazard ratios with 95% Wald-type confidence interval were estimated from weighted Cox proportional hazards models. Weighted Kaplan-Meier curves were applied to describe OS, where median survival with 95% CI was reported for each treatment group. Summary statistics of the baseline covariates for both cohorts were provided before and after adjustment. Other weighting schemes, e.g., average treatment effect for overlap population (ATO), average treatment effects on the treated (ATT) and average treatment effects on the control (ATC) were also presented for the propensity score method. Multivariate proportional hazards regression model including treatment and baseline covariates were also performed.

L987: ## Results/Discussion
L988: ## Part 1
L989: There were 426 patients with advanced bladder cancer represented in the Flatiron-FMI clinico-genomic database as of January 31, 2018 (data cutoff). Of 426 patients in the database, 103 patients did not have specific treatment information available. Among the remaining 323
L990: patients who received any treatment, 141 patients received anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy. Of the 141 patients, 17 patients were treated in the context of a clinical study and did not have specific treatment information available. Therefore, 124 patients were included in the analyses for anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy. Table 81 presents the demographics and baseline characteristics and Table 83 presents the baseline disease characteristics of patients who received any treatment (N=323) and patients who received any line of anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy (N=124). None of the patients had fusions involving FGFR2.

L1005: **Reviewer Comments:** Baseline demographic characteristics for the treated population of 323 patients who received any therapy and for the anti PD-L1/PD-1 population of 124 patients who received anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy in any line of therapy were balanced between FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative patients regarding age, sex, race and smoking history. However, due to substantial missing data, conclusions summarizing tumor stage, ECOG performance status, hemoglobin level, and PD-L1 expression are difficult to make. We note that no patients with FGFR2 alterations were included in this database.
L1006: There are, therefore, multiple limitations with the data on which the analyses presented are based. First, the size of the cohort is small, with only 27 patients with FGFR-positive tumors who received anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy. Second, there are several inherent limitations of real-world
L1007: *data that present challenges even with something as basic as accurately recording baseline characteristics.*
L1008: *EHRs often contain incomplete data. Determining the timing of a diagnosis from clinical data alone is challenging. Not every diagnosis is recorded at every visit. First diagnosis in a database is not necessarily an incident case of disease. Flatiron's database may not capture all therapies received by individual patients, as patients may seek care from providers that do not contribute to the Flatiron database. Thus, defining the number of lines of therapy that patients have previously received is problematic.*
L1009: *PD-L1 expression status, ECOG performance score, clinical stage, histology, chemotherapy regimen (dose and schedule), and other clinical factors cannot always be determined from the available data. Because of these issues and the resultant missing data, these important factors could not be incorporated into a multivariate model. This is especially important in survival analysis, as causality in survival is complicated, and death is affected by many socioeconomic factors that are not captured in EHR data. It is difficult to interpret OS results in a non-randomized setting as a retrospective study cannot measure all known or unknown prognostic factors.*
L1010: ## **Prognostic effect analysis results**
L1011: Of 323 patients treated with any treatment, 17 patients had last known alive date before FMI reporting date and did not contribute to the OS analysis. The prognostic value of FGFR alterations was assessed in patients treated with any line (N=306; 59 FGFR-positive, 247 FGFR-negative) or 2nd line (N=199; 37 FGFR-positive, 162 FGFR-negative) of therapy. Results of the OS comparison between FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative cohorts by line of therapies are summarized in
L1012: Table 83. Kaplan-Meier curves of OS by FGFR status are shown in Figure 7.

L1019: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation (NDA [NME] 212018)
## BALVERSA™ (erdafitinib)
L1020: [Source: Reviewer analysis using adsl.xpt and adtte.xpt datasets (Flatiron-FMI)]
L1021: ## **Reviewer Comments:**
L1022: As the OS comparison between FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative is not based on randomization, potential confounders would affect the comparison results from univariate Cox models. The applicant conducted multivariate analyses using a selected set of potential prognostic variables as covariates in Cox regression models. However, with small number of events in the FGFR-positive cohort, results from the multivariate Cox regression model adjusted by 7 covariates are problematic and the confidence interval estimates are not reliable. As shown in Figure 7, the Kaplan-Meier curves of FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative cohorts are close to each other. Though the point estimate of hazard ratio between FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative was higher than 1 favoring the FGFR-negative cohort, the confidence intervals were wide and covered 1. No definitive conclusion can be made from the Flatiron data about the prognostic impact of FGFR alterations in mUC patients who have received any line of therapy or 2nd line therapy.
L1023: ## **FGFR Alterations as Predictive Factor**
L1024: - OS with Anti PD-L1/PD-1 Therapy
L1025: Of 124 patients treated with any anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy, 6 patients had last known alive date before FMI reporting date and did not contribute to the OS analysis. The predictive value of FGFR alterations was assessed in patients who received anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy in any line (N=118; 26 FGFR-positive, 92 FGFR-negative), in 1st line (N=40; 12 FGFR-positive, 28 FGFR-negative), and/or in 2nd line (N=59; 12 FGFR-positive, 47 FGFR-negative). The hazard ratio of the OS comparison between FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative cohorts in patients receiving any line of anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy is 1.33 with a 95% CI from 0.78 to 2.26. The hazard ratio adjusted by the pre-specified baseline covariates using the multivariate Cox regression model is 1.25 (95% CI: 0.71, 2.21). The hazard ratios of the OS comparison in patients receiving anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy at 1st and 2nd line are 1.94 (95% CI: 0.78, 4.82) and 1.17 (95% CI: 0.53, 2.59), respectively.
L1026: ## - **OS with Chemotherapy**
L1027: Of 274 patients who treated with chemotherapy, 36 patients did not have specific treatment information available and 12 patients had last known alive date before FMI reporting date and did not contribute to the OS analysis. The predictive value of FGFR alterations was assessed in patients treated with chemotherapy in any line (N=224; 34 FGFR-positive, 190 FGFR-negative), in 1st line (N=209; 28 FGFR-positive, 181 FGFR-negative), and/or in 2nd line (N=97; 13 FGFR-positive, 84 FGFR-negative). The hazard ratio of the OS comparison between FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative cohorts in patients receiving any line of chemotherapy is 1.07 with a 95% CI from 0.69 to 1.66. The hazard ratios of the OS comparison in patients receiving chemotherapy at 1st and 2nd line are 0.96 (95% CI: 0.60, 1.54) and 2.08 (95% CI: 1.05, 4.13), respectively.
L1028: **Reviewer Comments:** As the OS comparison between FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative is not based on randomization, potential confounders would affect the comparison results from the Cox regression models. With small number of events in the FGFR-positive cohort and the lack of randomization in the study design, results from the multivariate Cox regression model adjusted by 7 covariates are problematic and the confidence interval estimates are not reliable.
L1029: Among patients treated with anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy, the point estimate for the hazard of death numerically favored patients without FGFR alterations, but the confidence interval was wide and included 1.0. Given the small sample size in the FGFR-positive cohort, imbalance between the two groups with respect to number of patients, and wide confidence interval observed for hazard ratio, no conclusion on the predictive value of FGFR alterations could be made in patients who received anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy or chemotherapy.
L1030: ## - **Real-world tumor response**
L1031: ## Anti PD-L1/PD-1 in any line of therapy
L1032: Of 141 patients treated with anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy in any line of therapy, 17 patients were treated in the context of a clinical study and did not have data available for rwTR assessment. Flatiron retrospectively abstracted response information for the remaining 124 anti PD-L1/PD-1 treated patients (27 FGFR-positive, 97-FGFR-negative) in the Flatiron-FMI database. The real world best tumor response data are summarized in Table 84.

L1035: CR = complete response; PR = partial response; SD = stable disease
L1036: 1FGFR-pos: FGFR-positive
L1037: 2FGFR-neg: FGFR-negative
L1038: [Source: Reviewer analysis using adsl.xpt and adtte.xpt datasets (Flatiron-FMI)]
L1039: **Reviewer Comments:** The response rates were comparable (approximately 20-22% of patients met rwBTR criteria for CR or PR) irrespective of FGFR status in patients who received anti PD-L1/PD-1 treatment in any line, with wide, overlapping confidence intervals (Table 84). The real world disease control rate were also comparable (30-31%, irrespective of FGFR status, based on our assessment of radiology and clinical reports). The Applicant acknowledged that interpretation of these response rates must be approached cautiously because rwBTR criteria are less stringent than RECIST criteria.
L1040: The original Flatiron-assessed rwDCR was 31/97 (34%) in the anti PD-L1/PD-1 treated, FGFR-negative patients. However, given the inherent subjectivity and variable application of real world radiologic assessment, FDA sent an IR to obtain patient-level clinical and radiologic
L1041: *response narratives. FDA review of data submitted in response revealed that two of the SD patients originally classified as having a rwBTR of SD had incomplete restaging to support an assessment of SD at any point. These two patients were re-categorized as PD in the analysis presented in Table 84.*
L1042: ## **FDA's overall conclusion on prognostic/predictive value of FGFR alterations (Part 1):**
L1043: In summary, with the analysis results observed and limitation of the dataset we cannot make any definitive conclusion on the prognostic or predictive impact of FGFR alterations in patients with advanced urothelial cancer.
L1044: ## **Part 2**
L1045: The Applicant compared overall survival among the FGFR-positive patients with advanced or metastatic urothelial cancer from the Phase 2 clinical study (BLC2001) who received erdafitinib, versus the FGFR-positive patients identified from the Flatiron-FMI database who received anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy or chemotherapy. Per the Applicant's analysis, the results suggested an improvement in overall survival in patients treated with erdafitinib in Regimen 3 (8 mg once daily) of BLC2001 when compared to those treated with anti PD-L1/PD-1 or chemotherapy in the real-world setting from Flatiron-FMI database. The point estimate of hazard ratios for the OS analysis based on propensity score weighting methodologies and multivariate regression were approximately 0.3 for different comparisons. The Applicant concluded that patients treated with erdafitinib in the BLC2001 Study had a survival benefit compared to FGFR-positive patients in the Flatiron-FMI dataset treated with anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapies or chemotherapy.
L1046: **Reviewer Comments:** *Several important methodological issues impact interpretability of these findings. These include 1) unmeasured confounding and substantial missingness of key confounding factors, e.g., ECOG was missing in approximately 30% of control patients; 2) more stringent exclusion criteria for trial patients such that these were more likely to be healthier than controls; 3) differential selection of comparison groups, specifically, that trial patients were enrolled from academic medical centers primarily from Europe, while controls were enrolled from community oncology centers in the US; 4) potential differential treatment misclassification; 5) incomplete capture of death information in controls, and 6) very small sample size (n=16\sim26) in the real-world control cohorts. Refer to the review conducted by Office of Surveillance and Epidemiology for additional details.*
L1047: ## **FDA's conclusion on indirect comparison of erdafitinib versus standard of care (Part 2):**
L1048: Because both internal and external validity are significantly impacted by these methodological issues, we do not consider these analyses sufficient for use as evidence supporting the effectiveness of the drug.
L1049: ## Bladder Cancer Research Initiative for Drug Targeting in Germany (BRIDGE) Database
L1050: ## Study Design
L1051: Clinical data were obtained from 5 BRIDGE consortium clinical sites (Universities of: Erlangen, Mannheim; Ulm, Regensburg, and Herne), and FGFR alteration screening was performed jointly by the Universities of Erlangen (mutations) and Mannheim (fusions). Study-eligible patients were those who:
L1052: - had sufficient tissue available for FGFR alteration testing
- were not treated in the context of a clinical study
L1053: ## Study Objectives
L1054: The primary objective was to determine the value of FGFR alterations in predicting anti PD-L1/PD-1 treatment outcome. PD-L1 expression and correlation with OS were also assessed.
L1055: ## Study Endpoints
L1056: Overall survival (OS) was defined as the start date of anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy to the date of death.
L1057: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L1058: Kaplan-Meier survival function estimation and Cox proportional hazard models were implemented in the analysis.
L1059: ## Results/discussion
L1060: Across clinical sites, 109 advanced or mUC patients (i.e., bladder and upper urinary tract) were identified who received anti PD-L1/PD-1 treatment on or after February 2016. Of those patients, 16 were excluded due to treatment in the context of a clinical study, and 30 had no tissue available or insufficient tissue for testing (n=28) or missing treatment start dates in the transferred data (n=2), who were also excluded. A total of 63 patients who had both clinical data and FGFR alteration status were included in the analysis. Results of the OS comparison between FGFR-positive and FGFR-negative cohorts are summarized in Table 85.

L1065: FDA requested the tumor response data since the BRIDGE dataset was more recent than the Flatiron-FMI dataset, and data were likely to be obtained in a more uniform and complete manner. The Applicant submitted the available response data for 57/63 patients in the BRIDGE dataset on December 27, 2018. RECIST-based response data were not available for the BRIDGE dataset. Response was determined from review of clinical records by investigators at each of 5 participating centers. Investigators were not given pre-defined criteria by which to assess response and were not required to provide an overall response determination. No guidelines were used to assess responses consistently across all 5 sites.
L1066: In the 57 patients with response data available, the real world tumor response rate (CR or PR) was reported as 38% (3/8) for FGFR-positive patients and 25% (12/49) for FGFR-negative patients, with large and overlapping confidence intervals (Table 86).

L1071: ## **Overall conclusion of RWE from the review team**
L1072: Given the analysis results observed and data limitations, no definitive conclusion can be made about the prognostic or predictive impact of FGFR alterations in patients with metastatic urothelial cancer.
L1073: ## **8.1.4. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness**
L1074: In NDA 212018, the Applicant has submitted substantial evidence of effectiveness in support of the approval of erdafitinib. This approval is primarily based on efficacy results of BLC2001, a single-arm, open-label, multi-center clinical study of erdafitinib, in which 87 patients were enrolled with locally advanced or metastatic urothelial carcinoma with susceptible FGFR3 and FGFR2 genetic alterations and who progressed during or following at least one line of prior
L1075: chemotherapy, including within 12 months of neoadjuvant adjuvant platinum-containing chemotherapy. These 87 patients all received erdafitinib at the proposed dose, which is 8mg daily with pharmacodynamically-guided dose increase to 9 mg based on serum phosphate and tolerability. The conduct of this non-randomized, single-arm clinical study is adequate to generate sufficient data to support the effectiveness of erdafitinib. The efficacy results of these 87 patients enrolled at the proposed dose and proposed disease setting in BLC2001 support the claim of clinical benefit through the surrogate endpoint of ORR, and this supports the decision to grant accelerated approval of erdafitinib for the treatment of adult patients with urothelial carcinoma which has susceptible FGFR3 or FGFR2 genetic alterations, and which has progressed during or following at least one line of prior platinum -containing chemotherapy, including within 12 months of neoadjuvant or adjuvant platinum-containing chemotherapy, under the provisions in 21 CFR Part 601 Subpart E (accelerated approval).
L1076: A confirmed ORR as assessed by BIRC per RECIST v1.1 of 32.2% (95%CI: 22.4, 42.0) was observed in patients receiving erdafitinib 8 mg once daily. The detected responses appeared durable with a duration of response of 5.4 months (95%CI: 41.2, 6.9 months). Subgroup analyses support the overall substantial evidence of effectiveness. Patients in all risk categories based on Bellmunt and MSKCC scores demonstrated responses on erdafitinib, as did patients with liver metastases. These results indicate a clinically meaningful benefit for patients in the indicated population in this setting.
L1077: In the second-line treatment of urothelial cancer, pembrolizumab is the standard of care with a demonstrated overall survival advantage over chemotherapy. Patients with FGFR-altered cancers are considered a genetically-defined subset of patients who may not respond as other patients would to available therapy. The ORR per BIRC reported by pembrolizumab in Keynote 361 is 21% (95% CI:16,27). While the confidence intervals for the ORRs for pembrolizumab and erdafitinib overlap, the review team believes that there is sufficient evidence to demonstrate clinical benefit. This molecularly-defined subgroup of patients with urothelial cancer that harbor FGFR genetic alterations also appears to have a very low reported response rate to anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy, as reported in BLC2001 with a reported response rate of 4.8%, in comparison to a reported response rate of 34.5% to prior chemotherapy. These patients who had for the most part not demonstrated a previous response to anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy had a 59% response rate to erdafitinib when treated on BLC2001. In contrast, attempts by the applicant to use real-world evidence from the Flatiron-FMI and BRIDGE consortium data to demonstrate that patients with urothelial cancer and FGFR alterations did less well than those without FGFR alterations in terms of response rate and overall survival to anti PD-L1/PD-1 directed therapies were hampered by methodologic flaws that made these analyses uninterpretable.
L1078: A review issue faced by the review team when considering the erdafitinib indication statement was whether to include all enrolled FGFR genetic alterations in the approved and labeled population. Generally, while FGFR genetic alterations occur in about 20% of patients with metastatic bladder cancer, the majority of alterations in this setting are FGFR point mutations,
L1079: specifically point mutations in FGFR3. FGFR fusions (of FGFR3 and FGFR2) are far less common in this setting. FGFR2 fusions specifically appear to be very rare in bladder cancer and it is unclear if they represent molecular “drivers” in this setting, although they appear to be of clinical importance in other settings such as hepatobiliary cancers.
L1080: The review team thus undertook several analyses of submitted data for this NDA to characterize the expected frequency of various FGFR molecular alterations in a metastatic urothelial cancer population. The first analysis was based on screened patients on BLC2001. Overall, 2214 patients were screened for molecular eligibility for enrollment for BLC2001, of which 417 patients had tissue specimens that were adequate for molecular screening and were found to carry an FGFR genetic alteration. This points to an approximately 18.8% overall incidence rate in this screened population. Data on the specific type of FGFR alteration present in each of the screened patients was not provided by the sponsor, but data was available for all 210 of those patients who were treated on BLC2001.

L1085: The team also reviewed real world evidence submitted with NDA 212018 from the Flatiron-FMI database to characterize the frequency of various FGFR alterations in these patients. In a cohort of 426 patients with advanced urothelial cancer and available Janssen genetic diagnostic results, FGFR3 alterations (FGFR3 point mutation or FGFR3 fusions) were identified in 79 (18.5%) patients. None of the patients had FGFR2 fusions. The table below summarizes the number of enrolled patients by FGFR alterations.

L1088: Based on review of the above data from BLC2001 and the Flatiron-FMI data, the majority of the ~20% of the patients with advanced FGFR-altered urothelial cancers have FGFR3 mutations (~80%). FGFR fusions appear to be much rarer, occurring in approximately 20% of these FGFR-altered patients, with most of these patients harboring FGFR3 fusions.
L1089: Molecular eligibility for BLC2001 included the presence of several prespecified FGFR genetic alterations, including several FGFR fusions (FGFR2-BICC1, FGFR2-CASP7, FGFR3-TACC3 [V1, V3], FGFR3-BAIAP2L1) as detected by a PCR-based clinical trial assay (CTA). These FGFR alterations were selected based on presumed frequency in bladder and/or other types of cancers and on
L1090: available nonclinical and clinical data at the time. On 12/04/2014, a pre-Phase 2 Meeting with the Sponsor was held to discuss the trial design of BLC2001, based on data from an ongoing Phase 1 study, 42756493EDI1001. The Sponsor cited preclinical studies as well as preliminary clinical findings from EDI1001 as the rationale for the FGFR genetic alterations to be used as an eligibility criterion for enrollment in BLC2001. Specifically, the FGFR fusions identified from the four patients in EDI1001 who achieved either a PR or SD (no patients achieved CR) and had urothelial carcinoma that had a FGFR fusion, responses of either PR or SD were observed in four patients with fusions: FGFR3-TACC3, FGFR2-BICC1/FGFR2-CASP7, FGFR3-BIAP2L1/ FGFR2-BICC1/FGFR2- CASP7/ FGFR2-OFD1, and FGFR3-TACC3/FGFR3 amp. These preliminary efficacy findings provided the primary clinical rationale for the selected FGFR genetic alterations included in BLC2001. In addition, the Sponsor also discussed the rationale for excluding other FGFR genetic alterations, such as FGFR gene amplifications and FGFR2-CCDC6 fusions, because of lack of sufficient clinical activity observed in the EDI1001. FDA agreed with these rationales, and thus patients with these prespecified FGFR fusions were enrolled on BLC2001.

The 8 mg dosing cohort is highlighted (n=87). Investigator-assessed response rates are presented for uniformity, as efficacy results of the 6 mg and 10 mg dosing cohorts were not independently reviewed.

L1096: Based on the above table, it is apparent that several types of FGFR fusions had no patients with responses observed on BLC2001 in any dosing cohort. One question faced by the review team was therefore whether to extend erdafitinib's indication to all patients with FGFR3 fusions, as responses were only observed in the most common type of FGFR3 fusion (FGFR3-TACC3_V1). A second question faced by the review team was whether to extend erdafitinib's indication to patients with FGFR2 fusions.
L1097: In terms of nonclinical data on FGFR fusions in bladder cancer, there is evidence of FGFR2 (and FGFR3 fusions) transforming in vitro, but not in urothelial cell lines. In bladder cancer models, 2 cell lines with FGFR3-TACC3 fusions demonstrated response to erdafitinib. No analogous data was submitted for FGFR2 fusions in bladder cancer models. Both FGFR2 and FGFR3 fusions were inhibited by erdafitinib in transfected cell-line models and in some xenograft models.
L1098: As can be seen in Table 89, the applicant reported one PR by investigator-assessed response that was observed in an FGFR2-BICC1 fusion positive patient in a different dosing cohort of erdafitinib (6mg, i.e. not part of the 87 patient primary efficacy population). This patient had nodal disease and a reported duration of response of 4.2 months. However, once the images of this patient were reviewed by an independent review committee (by request of FDA review team), the patient was found to have had a best overall response of PD, as a new lesion was seen at the first follow-up assessment by the independent reviewer. The applicant also provided data from the Phase 1 EDI1001 Study in which a patient with urothelial cancer and with an FGFR2-BICC1 fusion had an investigator-assessed PR with response duration of 7.2 months. However, review of this response revealed that the PR involved a single target lesion in the lung that decreased from a baseline value of 19 mm to a follow-up value of 4 mm. Central review of these images was not done on this phase 1 protocol and is no longer possible per the
L1099: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation (NDA [NME] 212018)
## BALVERSA™ (erdafitinib)
L1100: applicant due to absence of patient consent. There were also investigator-assessed PRs seen in patients with other tumor types with FGFR2 fusions- all PRs- in NSCLC, cervical cancer, and cholangiocarcinoma.
L1101: An additional consideration faced by the review team is that the companion diagnostic approved with erdafitinib has the following as a warning and precaution: FGFR2 fusions (FGFR2:BICC1 and FGFR2:CASP7) and FGFR3 fusion FGFR3:BAIAP2L1 not included in the intended use are also designed to be identified by the therascreen FGFR RGQ RT-PCR kit but have not been analytically and clinically validated using clinical specimens.

L1107: Ultimately, the review team concluded that per the recommendations in the Guidance for Industry- Developing Targeted Therapies in Low-Frequency Molecular Subsets of a Disease previously published by the FDA, mechanism-based evidence, non-clinical studies and clinical studies can be used as rationale to support an accelerated approval. Additionally, per the guidance, if the clinical trials are successful and other conditions for approval are met, the FDA
L1108: will, in most circumstances, approve the drug for all patients who meet the inclusion criteria for the trial based on the prespecified criteria, irrespective of the extent to which patients with various molecular alterations were represented in the clinical trial. Due to the rarity of FGFR fusions in this population, the subsets with no demonstrated responses on BLC2001 may be due to underrepresentation and low numbers of enrolled patients. The number of patients with FGFR fusions may be too small to exclude drug activity. The inclusion of these patients was agreed to in a pre-phase 2 meeting with the applicant. Additionally, the molecular eligibility criteria for the ongoing confirmatory clinical study BLC3001 include the same FGFR genetic alterations as were enrolled in BLC2001. The confirmation of clinical benefit in this small subgroup of patients in enrolled patients with FGFR fusions is part of the PMR related to submitting the BLC3001 results. Continued inclusion of these FGFR fusions in the erdafitinib indication statement at the time of BLC3001 submission will be a review issue.
L1109: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1110: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1111: The erdafitinib clinical safety database contains pooled data from a total of 416 patients in two phase 1 dose escalation trials in subjects with advanced solid tumors (EDI1001, and GAC1001) and one phase 2 trial (BLC2001) in patients with advanced or metastatic urothelial cancers. The proposed dose and regimen for approval is 8 mg daily with pharmacodynamically guided up-titration to 9 mg daily. A total of 99 subjects were treated with this dosing regimen in BLC2001. This includes the 87 patients who form the primary efficacy population for this NDA, as well as 12 patients who were treated in the first-line metastatic setting due to their cisplatin-ineligible status. Additionally, to expand the safety database of this dosing regimen, data from subjects in EDI1001 treated with 9-mg daily (no up-titration; n = 65) was also reviewed, for a total of 164 subjects treated with what is referred to as the 8/9 mg daily regimen. The Applicant coded verbatim adverse event (AE) terms for all three trials using MedDRA version 20.0.
L1112: **Reviewer's comment:** Because the intended patient population in this submission was relatively small (N = 87) the clinical review team examined the 8/9 mg daily population (N = 164) for a more in-depth review. The strategy of pooling of safety data for the 8/9 mg daily regimen from EDI1001 and BLC2001 is considered scientifically sound because the safety profile of erdafitinib is not thought to be markedly indication-dependent. This population forms the basis of the analyses for much of this safety review. For labeling purposes, the 87 patients with advanced urothelial cancer who form the primary efficacy (i.e. the indication) population for erdafitinib were used to inform safety analyses for section 6. The 87 patients were chosen for labeling purposes for clarity, to align with the efficacy population, and because the safety profile of erdafitinib in these patients was generally representative of the overall safety profile of the pooled analyses in the pooled 8/9 mg dosing cohort (n=164).

L1117: The safety data presented this review were submitted to the New Drug Application on September 18, 2018. The Applicant submitted a 90-Day Safety Update on December 18, 2018. The findings presented in the 90-Day Safety Update did not significantly change the overall safety conclusions.
L1118: ## 8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database
L1119: ## Overall Exposure
L1120: One hundred sixty-four subjects were treated with erdafitinib on the 8/9 mg daily regimen. At the time of clinical cut-off, 21 subjects (13%) were still receiving erdafitinib and 143 (87%) had discontinued treatment. The most common reason for treatment discontinuation was progressive disease, reported for 82 of subjects who discontinued treatment. Adverse events led to treatment discontinuation for 11.2% of subjects who discontinued treatment.
L1121: Of the 416 patients who received erdafitinib across all doses, 25 (6%) were still on treatment as of the clinical cut-off, and 391 (94%) had discontinued treatment. Median time on study (including follow-up after treatment discontinuation) was 8.2 months. Progressive disease led to treatment discontinuation for 83.9% of subjects who discontinued treatment, and adverse events led to treatment discontinuation for 9.2% of subjects. Death was the reason for discontinuation for 4 subjects: 3 subjects in Study EDI1001 (Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), sepsis; Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), pulmonary edema; and Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), pneumonia aspiration) and 1 subject in BLC2001 (Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), sepsis).

L1131: **Reviewer's comment:** Although median treatment durations overall were relatively short, the availability of safety information for patients treated &gt;6 months, and in particular, data from 30 patients in the pooled safety population treated for \ge 12 months, was considered adequate to
L1132: *inform the safety analysis for the accelerated approval of erdafitinib. It is for this reason that safety data was analyzed in detail for patients at all dose levels (n=416) in this review.*
L1133: ## **Relevant characteristics of the safety population:**
L1134: For the 8/9-mg daily regimen, the median age was 63.5 years. Seventy-five subjects (45.7%) were 65 years or older. Females were 37.2% of the population and males were 62.8%. Most subjects were white (78.7%), 31.1% were from the United States, 66.9% were from Europe, and 3.0% were from Asia. Common primary tumor sites were urothelial (67.1%), breast (11.0%), and reproductive system (6.7%). Baseline Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) scores were mostly 0 (45.1%) or 1 (50.0%). These subject characteristics were similar for all doses combined and for the 87 patients in the indication population.

L1141: One hundred and twenty-eight subjects (30.8%) in the total safety database had mild renal insufficiency (creatinine clearance &lt; 60 to ≥ 30 mL/min), including 15 patients (11.7%) with urothelial carcinoma who were cisplatin-ineligible.
L1142: **Reviewer's comment:** This relatively high percentage of patients with decreased baseline renal function likely reflects the advanced cancer population enrolled.
L1143: One hundred fifty-three (93.3%) subjects on the 8/9 mg daily regimen received prior systemic chemotherapy, mostly cisplatin (50.0%), gemcitabine (50.0%), and carboplatin (40.9%; subjects may have received multiple prior therapies), consistent with the high number of primary urothelial cancers. Prior anti PD-L1/PD-1 therapy was reported for 13.4% of subjects, endocrine therapy for 10.4% of subjects, and investigational drugs for 7.3%. Similar prior use of antineoplastic agents was reported across all dose regimens (data not shown).
L1144: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L1145: The size of the safety database and duration of erdafitinib exposure were sufficient to characterize the safety of erdafitinib for this NDA.
L1146: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1147: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1148: The submission contains all required components of the eCTD. The overall quality and integrity of the application appear acceptable.
L1149: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L1150: Class effects of FGFR inhibitors include eye disorders, nail and skin disorders, and hyperphosphatemia. In addition, arrhythmia-related TEAEs were considered adverse events of interest based on preclinical findings. Specific groupings of PTs included for each of these categories were outlined in the Statistical Analysis Plan for the SCS.
L1151: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1152: Safety testing of patients enrolled in the clinical trials included adverse events, clinical hematology and chemistry tests, urinalysis, vital sign measurements, physical examinations, ECGs, and ophthalmologic examinations. The latter were to include assessment of visual acuity, tonometry, central and peripheral fundoscopy, and optical coherence tomography (OCT). Treatment-emergent safety reporting periods were defined as the time from the first dose of study drug through the safety follow-up visit, initiation of cytotoxic chemotherapy or an investigational agent, or 30 days after the last dose of study drug.
L1153: **Reviewer's comment:** The routine clinical testing of patients enrolled in the clinical trials appears to have been adequate.
L1154: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1155: ## Deaths
L1156: Overall, 58 subjects of the 416 erdafitinib-treated patients (13.9%) died within 30 days of their last dose of study drug, 47 (11.3%) due to progressive disease. In the 8/9 mg daily population, 18 subjects (11.0%) died within 30 days of the last dose, 15 (9.1%) due to progressive disease.
L1157: **Table 96: Deaths within 30 Days of Last Dose (Safety Population)**
L1158: Attribution	Indication Population (n = 87)	8/9 mg QD plus 9 mg QD Safety Pop. (n = 164)	Total (n = 416)
