L1160: **Reviewer's comment:** The fatal adverse event in the indication population was a myocardial infarction (see below). The narratives for all patients with deaths within 30 days of last dose were reviewed and the reviewer agreed with the investigator's attribution of death in these cases. One patient (Subject (b) (6)) in the indication population experienced a fatal adverse event (myocardial infarction) one day after the last dose of study drug. Several narratives for these patients are presented in greater detail below:
L1161: The following narratives provide further detail on three patients in the 8/9 mg daily group who had fatal adverse events within 30 days of the last dose of study drug.
L1162: - Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) (Study EDI1001) was a 52-year-old female who entered the study with urothelial cancer and a history of anemia. On Study Day 270, one day after the last dose of study drug, she had a headache, which quickly progressed to a coma. A CT scan showed brain metastasis with hemorrhage. The following day, she died due to cerebral hemorrhage.
- Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) (Study EDI1001) was a 52-year-old male who entered the study with head and neck neuroendocrine carcinoma metastatic to cervical and retropharyngeal nodes, parotid gland, anterior nasal passage, and ethmoid sinus. On Study Day 17, the subject presented to the emergency room with Grade 3 tumor hemorrhage and Grade 4 sepsis. On Study Day 21, he was unstable with hypotension, hypoxia, and fever. The surgical oncologist stated that there was no surgical option. On Study Day 25, the subject died due to sepsis.
- Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) (Study BLC2001) was a 77-year-old male with a history of hyperlipidemia, diabetes mellitus, pulmonary embolism and myocardial infarction. On Day 100 he was hospitalized with Grade 3 urinary tract infection. While hospitalized, on Day 102, a Grade 3 acute myocardial infarction was reported. Treatment included acetylsalicylic acid and furosemide. Study drug was interrupted that day and was restarted at the same dose (9 mg/day) on Day 108. The subject received the last dose of the study drug on Day 120. On Day 121, the events of urinary tract infection and acute myocardial infarction resolved, and the subject was discharged. On the day of discharge, after arriving at home, he felt dizzy and was shortly pulseless and apneic. Resuscitation attempts were unsuccessful. It was suspected that he had a Grade 5 acute myocardial infarction.
L1163: The following narratives provide additional detail regarding two patients treated with other dosing regimens who had fatal adverse events which investigators considered to be at least possibly related to erdafitinib:
L1164: - Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) (10 mg intermittent regimen; Study EDI1001) was a 66-year-old female who entered the study with glioblastoma multiforme. On Study Day 15, the subject, who was on enoxaparin, was diagnosed with an SAE of Grade 2 intracranial hemorrhage, as well as Grade 3 hemiparesis, Grade 3 dehydration, Grade 3 muscle twitching, Grade 2 increased intracranial pressure, and Grade 2 seizures. The subject was transferred to the hospital for neurological intervention, and the following day, was placed in hospice care. On Study Day 20, the subject died from intracranial hemorrhage, which the investigator considered possibly related to study drug.
L1165: - Subject (b)(6) (10 mg intermittent regimen; Study EDI1001) was a 52-year old male who entered the study with head and neck cancer. Medical history included hypomagnesemia, hypothyroidism controlled, anemia, and tumor pain. On Study Day 40, 19 days after the last dose of study drug, he experienced Grade 4 tumor hemorrhage, was admitted to the hospital, and died from tumor bleeding that same day. Although the hemorrhage was listed as the probable cause of death, the investigator suspected the underlying tumor rather than erdafitinib to be the cause of the hemorrhage.
L1166: **Reviewer's comment:** Four of these deaths were attributed to tumor bleeding. Tumor bleeding is discussed further in Section 8.5.6 of this review.
L1167: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1168: Serious TEAEs occurred in 67 subjects (40.9%) on the 8/9 mg daily regimen, with dyspnea and general physical health deterioration being most common. For all doses combined, serious TEAEs occurred in 180 subjects (43.3%) with general physical health deterioration the most common (4.6%).

L1204: ## Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L1205: All 164 subjects treated on the 8/9-mg daily regimen experienced at least one treatment emergent adverse event (TEAE). Grade 3 or 4 TEAEs were experienced by 109 subjects (66.5%), with 10 (6.1%) reporting Grade 4 events. Across all doses, 415 subjects (99.8%) experienced at least one TEAE, and 253 (60.8%) experienced Grade 3 or 4 TEAEs.
L1206: For the 8/9-mg daily regimen, hyperphosphatemia was the most frequent TEAE (81.7%), followed by stomatitis (56.1%), dry mouth (45.7%), diarrhea (41.5%), decreased appetite (37.8%), constipation (35.4%), dysgeusia (35.4%), and dry skin (31.7%). The results were similar for all doses combined.

L1223: The Applicant provided a narrative summary of Subject [REDACTED](b) (6), who experienced Grade 4 neutropenia on study. Erdafitinib was discontinued on Day 168 due to disease progression. On Day 181, the subject was hospitalized due to Grade 4 septic shock, and on Day 187, he died of septic shock.
L1224: **Reviewer's comment:** Grade 4 neutropenia in Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) resolved to Grade 0 the following day, suggesting a spurious laboratory value. This neutropenia resolved 124 days before the patient developed Grade 4 sepsis, and therefore did not appear to be causally related.

L1240: - Subject (b)(6) was a 68-year-old male assigned to erdafitinib 8 mg once daily. His medical history included anorexia and general pain. Treatment was ongoing as of Day 223.

L1244: ## Vital Signs
L1245: No clinically meaningful changes in vital signs were reported during the two phase 1 trials. In BLC2001, for the 8-mg daily regimen, median increases from baseline to the highest point on
L1246: treatment were 3.1% for diastolic blood pressure and 2.1% for systolic blood pressure. Median decreases from baseline to the lowest point on treatment were 16% and 14%, respectively. For subject weight, the median maximum decrease during the study was 5.3%, and the median maximum increase was 0.8%.
L1247: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1248: ECGs, echocardiographic and MUGA findings, and arrhythmia-related adverse events are discussed in Section 8.2.5 of this review.
L1249: ## QT
L1250: The Applicant performed an exposure-response analysis of time-matched QTc and Holter data from patients on part 1 of Study ED11001. The highest dose evaluated was 12 mg once daily, which covered steady state exposure at the maximum therapeutic dose of 9 mg daily. No placebo or positive control was used. The CDER Interdisciplinary Review Team for QT Studies reviewed these data and concluded that erdafitinib had no large effect (i.e., &gt;20 ms) on QTc. See Section 8.5.3 of this review for details.
L1251: ## Immunogenicity
L1252: The review of clinical safety raised no concerns of immunogenicity with this small molecule. No formal immunogenicity studies were required or were done.
L1253: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1254: Eye disorders, nail and skin disorders, and hyperphosphatemia are class effects of FGFR inhibitors and were therefore considered adverse events of special interest (AESI). In addition, arrhythmia-related TEAEs were considered AESIs based on preclinical findings.
L1255: ## Retinal pigment epithelium detachment/central serous retinopathy
L1256: ## Background
L1257: Retinal pigment epithelial detachment (RPED) is defined as separation between the RPE and the inner aspect of Bruch's membrane. The space created by this separation is occupied by blood, serous exudate, drusenoid material, and/or fibrovascular tissue.
L1258: RPED in the general population may be inflammatory, ischemic, degenerative, or idiopathic, and most commonly occurs a part of age-related macular degeneration. The prototype idiopathic RPED is central serous retinopathy (CSR). RPED typically presents clinically as painless blurred vision and/or visual field defect, and its fundoscopic and fluorescein angiographic features vary according to the etiology.
L1259: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation (NDA [NME] 212018)
## BALVERSA™ (erdafitinib)
L1260: The mitogen activated protein kinase (MEK) inhibitors (e.g., trametinib, cobimetinib, binimetinib) are a class of small molecule drugs associated with retinopathy. In the trials supporting approval of the MEK inhibitors, subjects underwent ophthalmological examination, including fundoscopy and OCT, pretreatment and at regular intervals during treatment. Symptomatic or asymptomatic CSR was identified in up to 26% of subjects. Most patients were asymptomatic. Symptoms when present included reduced visual acuity, dyschromatopsia, photophobia, shadows, and glare. This reviewer is not aware of any reports of permanent ocular sequelae in patients who experienced RPED due to MEK inhibitors. The prescribing information for MEK inhibitors recommends that patients undergo ophthalmological evaluation at regular intervals and for new or worsening visual disturbances, and that treatment be withheld, dose reduced, or discontinued based on severity of retinopathy.
L1261: The CTCAE criteria, widely used for AE reporting in oncology trials, include a 4-category grading schema for retinopathy according to symptom severity (Table 105).
L1262: **Table 104: Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events. CTCAE, v5.0 – Retinopathy Classification**
L1263: Grade	Findings
1	Asymptomatic
2	Moderate decrease in visual acuity (20/40 or better or ≤ 3 lines from baseline)
3	Marked decrease in visual acuity (worse than 20/40 or > 3 lines from baseline)
4	Blindness (20/200 or worse in the affected eye)
L1264: ## *Incidence of CSR Events in the Safety Database*
L1265: The GAC1001, EDI1001, and BLC2001 clinical protocols required Amsler grid testing (a grid of horizontal and vertical lines used to evaluate central visual fields) for all subjects at screening, at the beginning of each treatment cycle, and at the end of treatment. A full ophthalmic examination was to be performed at screening and in the event of newly positive Amsler grid test results or new clinical symptoms.
L1266: **Reviewer's Comment:** The FDA ophthalmology consultant noted that the case report form for BLC2001 was not optimal and did not capture all information needed to adequately evaluate RPED events. For example:
L1267: - The case report form should record visual acuity as numeric values for each eye.
- Routine tonometry is not necessary.
- The case report form should ideally have included fundoscopic findings, findings from the slit lamp examination, and results of OCT. RPED events should ideally be monitored at approximately 2-3 week intervals (the BLC2001 protocol specified no monitoring frequency).
- Amsler grid testing provides an evaluation of central macular distortion. It is a poor screening test for RPED.
L1268: The Applicant considered the following MedDRA Preferred terms under the category of RPED/CSR: retinal detachment, vitreous detachment, retinal edema, retinopathy,
L1269: chorioretinopathy, detachment of RPE, and detachment of macular RPE. For the 8/9-mg daily regimen, 26 (15.9%) of subjects experienced one or more treatment-emergent RPED/CSR events, most commonly chorioretinopathy, retinal detachment, and detachment of RPE.

L1274: **Reviewer's comment:** Retinal detachment, chorioretinopathy, detachment of RPE, retinopathy, and detachment of macular RPE likely represent the same or similar events. Vitreous detachment and retinal edema are likely separate events from each other and separate from the RPED events described by the other terms.
L1275: ## Relationship between Prior Retinal Disease and Treatment-Emergent CSR Events
L1276: In BLC2001, 14 (6.7%) subjects had a history of macular or retinal problems. During the study, 2 (13.3%) of these subjects experienced retinal AEs (1 RPED and 1 retinal tear; Table 107). The grade of the retinal problem at baseline was not reported in these two subjects, so it is unclear whether their CSR events were treatment-emergent. In contrast 196 (93.3%) subjects had no prior history of retinal disease, and of them, 37 (18.9%) experienced treatment-emergent CSR events (data not shown).

L1280: ## *Time Course and Outcome of Treatment-Emergent CSR Events*
L1281: The median time to first onset of any Grade CSR event was 55 days in the 8-9 mg group and 57 days for all dose regimens combined. Dosing was reduced, interrupted, or discontinued for all subjects with CSR events, per protocol. In 7 of 26 subjects whose doses were interrupted or reduced, the CSR event resolved but later recurred; all recurrences were Grade 1 or 2. Three subjects in the 8/9 mg daily group and 6 subjects across all regimens discontinued erdafitinib due to CSR events.

L1291: The following narratives provide further detail on the three Grade 3 CSR events reported in patients on the 8/9 mg daily regimen:
L1292: - Subject (b) (6) was a 73-year-old male with no relevant medical history, who on Study Day 207 experienced a SAE of Grade 3 retinopathy. Ocular events reported prior to retinopathy were increased lacrimation, dry eye, eye pain, and blepharitis, all Grade 1, treated with clotrimazole and other ophthalmologicals. Upon occurrence of retinopathy, the subject was also discovered to have progressive disease, because of which, erdafitinib was discontinued. The event of retinopathy resolved on Study Day 316. At the time of clinical cutoff, the event of blepharitis was reported as not resolved.
- Subject (b) (6) was a 65-year-old male with no relevant medical history, who on Study Day 57 experienced a serious TEAE of Grade 3 detachment of retinal pigment epithelium and a non-serious TEAE of Grade 2 vision blurred. No concomitant medications were reported for these events. Treatment with erdafitinib was interrupted due to the detachment of retinal pigment epithelium. On Study Day 139, the detachment of retinal pigment epithelium and vision blurred were reported. On Study Day 165, detachment of retinal pigment epithelium (Grade 2) recurred. Erdafitinib was interrupted on Study Day 166 and was subsequently discontinued due to the detachment of retinal pigment epithelium.
- Subject (b) (6) was a 63-year-old male with no relevant medical history, who on Study Day 87 experienced a serious TEAE of Grade 3 retinal edema, treated with hyaluronate sodium and retinol. Erdafitinib had been interrupted on Study Day 81 due to non-serious Grade 1 renal impairment and Grade 2 onycholysis. Other ocular events reported prior to retinal edema were Grade 1 vision blurred and Grade 2 conjunctivitis. On Study Day 115, the retinal edema improved to Grade 1. Erdafitinib was resumed on Study Day 130 at 5 mg daily. On 165, erdafitinib was discontinued due to disease progression. On Study Day 199, the retinal edema resolved.
L1293: ## **Reviewer's comments:**
L1294: - Approximately 16% of subjects treated with erdafitinib at 8-9 mg daily experienced one or more RPED/CSR events, with a median time to first onset of 55 days. All three Grade 3 RPED/CSR events improved to Grade 2 (n = 1) or Grade 0 (n = 2) by the clinical cutoff date.
- No subject developed tearing of the RPE (RPE tears, usually at the junction between the detached and attached portions of the RPE, with a marked decrease in visual acuity, have been reported in other types or RPED) or retinal vein occlusion (reported in some patients receiving MEK inhibitors).
- A prior history of retinal disease was not associated with an increased risk of treatment-emergent CSR events. Therefore, exclusion criteria pertaining to history of CSR, RVO, AMD, diabetic retinopathy, uncontrolled glaucoma, or corneal pathology need not be retained as contraindications for use of erdafitinib.

L1303: **Reviewer's comment:** Although listed separately, lacrimation is often a response to the dry eye events listed above.
L1304: Five subjects on the 8/9 mg daily regimen had ≥ Grade 3 non-CSR ocular events of keratitis (n = 3), dry eye, visual acuity reduced, corneal erosion, and cataract (n = 1 each). One subject on the 8-9 mg daily regimen experienced a Grade 4 non-CSR ocular event (cataract).
L1305: - Subject (b)(6) was a 65-year-old male who on Day 57 experienced non-serious Grade 2 vision blurred and Grade 3 SAE of detachment of retinal pigment epithelium. Treatment was interrupted. On Study Day 60, he complained of decreased visual acuity and dry syndrome. Visual acuity was 4/10. Fundoscopy and OCT showed bilateral pigmentary epithelial detachment. No treatment was reported for these events. Repeat OCT on Day 81 revealed right eye 288 µm and left eye 282 µm. On an unspecified date, ophthalmological examination was normal. On Day 139, the detachment of retinal pigment epithelium and vision blurred resolved. On Day 144, erdafitinib was resumed at 6 mg once daily. On Day 165, a non-serious AE of Grade 2 detachment of retinal pigment epithelium was reported. No treatment was reported for this event. Erdafitinib was discontinued.
- Subject (b)(6) was a 63-year-old male who on Day 22 experienced non-serious Grade 1 vision blurred. No action was taken with the study drug. On Day 28, visual acuity, tonometry, and fundoscopy, and OCT were normal in both eyes. On Day 43, the event of vision blurred resolved. On an unspecified date during Month 2 of treatment, Grade 2 conjunctivitis was reported. Treatment included carmellose and cethexonium, and no action was taken with the study drug. On Day 81, erdafitinib was interrupted due to non-ocular AEs. On Day 85, the conjunctivitis improved to Grade 1. On Day 87, Grade 3 retinal edema was reported. OCT was abnormal (thickness 180 µm in the right eye and 331µm in the left eye). Erdafitinib continued to be held, and the subject was treated with hyaluronate and retinol. On Day 98, the conjunctivitis resolved, a Grade 2 cataract was reported, OCT showed thickness of 247 µm in the right eye (normal) and 276 µm in the left eye (abnormal), no action was taken with erdafitinib, and treatment included hyaluronate, retinol, carmellose, and cethexonium. On Day 115, the retinal edema improved to Grade 1. On Day 130, erdafitinib was resumed at 5 mg daily, Grade 1 dry eye was reported, no action was taken with the study drug, and no treatment was reported. On Day 166, erdafitinib was interrupted due to non-ocular AEs. On Day 168, the dry eye resolved. On Day 169, erdafitinib was discontinued due to progressive disease. On Day 199, the event of retinal edema resolved. At the data cutoff date, the events of cataract and pneumothorax had not resolved.
L1306: Approximately two thirds of subjects on the 8-9 mg regimen who had non-CSR ocular events continued erdafitinib treatment without dose modification. At the time of data cutoff, 31 subjects (42.5%) had ongoing non-CSR ocular events.

L1310: **Reviewer's comment:** Events of dry eye syndrome are frequent during treatment with erdafitinib. Dry eye is also frequently associated with aging. The Applicant recommends the use of artificial tear substitutes, hydrating or lubricating eye gels or ointments as routine prophylaxis for dry eye syndrome during the entire treatment period at least every 2 hours while the patient is awake. The FDA ophthalmology consultant concurred that all patients should receive prophylaxis with ocular demulcents as needed; however, recommendations to use lubricating eye gels are discouraged since there are no FDA-approved dry eye therapy gels.

L1330: ## Arrhythmias
L1331: Erdafitinib is a human ether-a-go-go-related gene (hERG) blocker, and erdafitinib prolonged repolarization in guinea pigs and dogs. Because of this preclinical signal, arrhythmias were identified as of potential clinical importance.
L1332: ## Electrocardiography
L1333: In the phase 1 trials GAC1001 and EDI1001, all subjects had electrocardiography at screening; predose and 2, 4, and 8 hours postdose on Days 1, 8, and 15 of Cycle 1 and on Day 1 of Cycle 2; and postdose on Day 1 of subsequent cycles.
L1334: In GAC1001, no subject had a QTc value &gt; 500 msec or a \DeltaQTc &gt; 60 msec from baseline.
L1335: In EDI1001, 19 subjects (10.1%) had average maximum \DeltaQTcF values 30 to 60 msec, and 27 subjects (14.4%) had maximum average \DeltaQTcB values 30 to 60 msec. Average QTc values exceeded 500 msec on treatment in 2 subjects (Subjects [b] (6)), both of whom has screening values above 500 msec. Four subjects had electrocardiogram QT prolonged reported as a TEAE (Table 112). None of these events was considered serious, and none of these four subjects had other arrhythmia-related adverse events.
L1336: **Table 115: TEAEs of Electrocardiogram QT Prolonged**

L1339: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation (NDA [NME] 212018)
## BALVERSA™ (erdafitinib)
L1340: Based on these findings, the phase 2 trial BLC2001 collected ECGs at screening only in subjects on the 8 mg daily regimen. In this group, no QTcF values &gt; 500 msec, \DeltaQTcF &gt; 60 msec or arrhythmia-related TEAEs were reported.
L1341: ## *Echocardiography*
L1342: In the dose-escalation part of the phase 1 trial EDI1001, echocardiography or multigated acquisition (MUGA) was performed at screening and in Cycle 3, Week 1. The left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF) was &gt; 50% at screening in all subjects. Of thirty-five subjects who had one or more post-baseline assessments, three had a maximum decrease in LVEF of 5-6%. No subject experienced a post-baseline decrease in LVEF &gt; 6% or a post-baseline LVEF value below 55%.
L1343: In the phase 2 trial BLC2001, all subjects underwent echocardiography or MUGA at screening. Two subjects on the 8-mg regimen were enrolled despite not meeting the LVEF eligibility criterion of \ge 50%. Subsequent echocardiogram or MUGA assessments were to be done at the discretion of the investigator, and no subject required either study for suspected cardiac issues.
L1344: ## *Adverse Events*
L1345: The Applicant considered the following MedDRA Preferred terms arrhythmia related: ventricular fibrillation, ventricular flutter, ventricular tachyarrhythmia, ventricular tachycardia, electrocardiogram QT prolonged, bradycardia, atrial fibrillation, atrial flutter, loss of consciousness, syncope, and ventricular arrhythmia. Five subjects (3.0%) on the 8/9 mg daily regimen experienced arrhythmia-related TEAEs, two of which were Grade 3 (both syncope); no Grade 4 events were reported. No arrhythmia-related event was serious or resulted in dose reduction, interruption, or discontinuation.
L1346: Across all dose regimens, 15 subjects (3.6%) had arrhythmia-related TEAEs, with a median time to first onset of 20.0 days. Two subjects had dosing interrupted as a result, and 1 subject discontinued erdafitinib.

L1350: The following narratives provide further detail on select subjects with arrhythmia-related events:
L1351: - Subject (b) (6), a 63-year-old male on the 8-9 mg daily regimen, had Grade 1 ventricular arrhythmia that was reported on Study Day 1 and persisted throughout his study participation. The subject had a history of hypertension, and his screening ECG showed intraventricular conduction delay, ventricular premature complexes, and a QTc of 412 msec. His Day 1 ECG showed first degree atrioventricular block and a QTcB of 450 msec. The subject continued study treatment without further cardiac events until Day 163, when he discontinued treatment due to disease progression (his event of syncope on Day 141 occurred with groin pain and urinary retention).
- Subject (b) (6), a 71-year-old male with a history of anemia and hypertension on the 6-mg daily regimen, had a fatal event of loss of consciousness at home 3 days after erdafitinib was discontinued because of progressive disease. No autopsy was performed.
- Subject (b) (6) was an 82-year-old male assigned to erdafitinib 8 mg daily. Medical history included angina pectoris, atrial fibrillation, hypertension, myocardial infarction, and ventricular ectopy. Concomitant medications included aspirin, amiodarone, atenolol, ramipril, simvastatin, salbutamol, tiotropium, levothyroxine, and furosemide. On Study Day -1, non-serious Grade 3 hyponatremia was reported. No treatment was reported for this event. On Day 24, non-serious Grade 1 diarrhea was reported and was treated with loperamide. No action was taken with the study drug. On Day 26, the diarrhea resolved. On Day 28, the hyponatremia worsened to Grade 4. On Day 35 the subject was hospitalized. On Day 36, erdafitinib was interrupted due to hyponatremia and was never resumed. On Day 38, non-serious Grade 3 syncope was reported. The subject received oxygen. The event of syncope resolved the same day. On Day 77, the
L1352: hyponatremia improved to Grade 3 and was considered non-serious. On Day 168, the hyponatremia resolved. Concurrent AEs included eye pain, hyperphosphatemia, insomnia, conjunctivitis, and stomatitis (all Grade 1).
L1353: - Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) was a 65-year-old Asian male with metastatic lung cancer previously treated with pemetrexed, carboplatin, bevacizumab. His medical history included hyponatremia, elevated alkaline phosphate, and exertional dyspnea. Grade 1 ALT was present at screening. The subject was assigned to erdafitinib 12 mg 7 days on/7 days off. On Study Day 1, he had SAEs of Grade 4 hyponatremia, Grade 3 tachycardia, and Grade 3 atrial flutter. Two hours after his first dose of erdafitinib, he felt nauseous and vomited. He received ondansetron without recurrence. His 8 hour post-dose EKG showed a heart rate of 161. This was treated with diltiazem, digoxin, acetylsalicylic acid, and adenosine, and erdafitinib was interrupted. Concomitant medications included magnesium sulfate, sodium chloride, enoxaparin, and tolvaptan. On Day 3, the subject was in sinus rhythm. On Day 6, he was discharged from the hospital. On Day 8, erdafitinib was resumed at 12 mg. On Day 11, the subject experienced SAEs of atrial flutter and tachycardia and Grade 3 anemia, treated with oxygen, diltiazem, sodium chloride, and whole blood. Erdafitinib was interrupted, and the heart rhythm converted to normal sinus. On Day 14, Grade 3 ALT and Grade 3 AST were reported. Erdafitinib continued to be held. On Day 29, AST and ALT resolved to Grade 1, and erdafitinib was resumed at 8 mg intermittent. On Day 35, ALT increased to Grade 2, and erdafitinib was interrupted. On Day 43, these resolved to Grade 1. On Day 45, erdafitinib was resumed at 8 mg. On Day 50, per protocol, a drug holiday of 7 days ended the cycle. On Day 55, the subject was hospitalized because of Grade 3 acute kidney injury and Grade 4 hyponatremia, and concomitant AEs included Grade 2 renal tubular necrosis, Grade 2 atrial flutter, Grade 3 congestive heart failure, Grade 2 hypervolemia, Grade 2 restrictive cardiomyopathy, and Grade 2 malnutrition. Chest radiography showed worsening left pleural effusion and opacity. Broad spectrum antibiotics were administered. Echocardiography showed a decrease in LVEF to 35%, with tumor infiltrating the heart. He had been off rate controlling agents for his atrial flutter and went into rapid atrial flutter on Day 65. He was treated with amiodarone. On Day 69, the subject was transferred to hospice care, and on Day 71, he died of disease progression. No autopsy was performed.
L1354: **Reviewer's comment:** The patients described in these narratives all had alternative causes for their arrhythmia-related events, and some of those causes seemed more likely than erdafitinib. This will need to be further assessed in the ongoing randomized trial.
L1355: ## **Exposure-Response Analysis of Cardiovascular Safety**
L1356: Holter recordings were obtained in Part 1 of Study EDI1001 at screening, on Cycle 1 Day 1, and at steady state on Cycle 2 Day 1, and Cycle 3 Day 1. ECGs were obtained at the same times as total and free erdafitinib plasma concentrations. The dataset includes 62 subjects Holter/ECGs
L1357: paired with 704 total erdafitinib concentrations, and 60 subjects with Holter/ECGs paired with 685 free erdafitinib concentrations. The QT interval corrected individually for heart rate (QTcI) was selected as the dependent variable.
L1358: The Applicant's exposure-response analysis estimated the slopes for total and free erdafitinib plasma concentrations as -0.00269 ms/(ng/mL) and -1.138 ms/(ng/mL), respectively, indicating that increases in erdafitinib plasma concentrations result in decreased QTcI. The model-predicted mean \DeltaQTcI at the observed geometric mean of the C_{max} for total (1911 ng/mL) and free (4.51 ng/mL) plasma concentration at steady state following a 9 mg dose of erdafitinib was -5.1 msec (90% CI: -8.8, -1.5) and -5.1 msec (90% CI: -8.3, -2.0), respectively.
L1359: ## Hyperphosphatemia
L1360: ## Laboratory Values
L1361: Because hyperphosphatemia is a pharmacodynamic effect of FGFR inhibitors, protocols EDI1001 and BLC2001 directed that erdafitinib be up-titrated from 8 to 9 mg in some cohorts for phosphate levels below 5.5 mg/dL, in the absence of drug-related toxicity. The dose was to be reduced in all cohorts if serum phosphate levels exceeded prespecified values.
L1362: Hyperphosphatemia was also managed by dose modification and treatment with phosphate binders (although the protocols provided no guidance regarding the use of phosphate binders). Treatment with phosphate binders used included sevelamer (26 subjects), sevelamer carbonate (5 subjects), and lanthanum carbonate, calcium carbonate, and calcium acetate (1 subject each). Phosphate binders were used prophylactically for 5 subjects and for 29 subjects following phosphate assessment during the study. The median total duration of phosphate binder treatment was 81 days. The median phosphate value prompting phosphate binder treatment was 6.10 mg/dL
L1363: In the 8-9 mg daily group and in all groups combined, mean phosphate values increased starting at Week 2, peaked by Week 6, and decreased from that point forward. During treatment, 132 (81.0%) subjects on the 8/9-mg daily regimen had phosphate increases of Grade 1 or 2, and 2 subjects (1.2%) had increases to Grade 3. Across all regimens, 282 (68.5%) subjects had phosphate increases to Grade 1 or 121 values, and 3 subjects (0.7%) had shifts to Grade 3 post-baseline. No Grade 4 values were reported.

All (n = 412)	132 (32.0%)	189 (45.9%)	88 (21.4%)	3 (0.67%)	0
L1366: Source: dataset ADSL; variables: USUBJID, TR01AG1, TEMFL, AEDECOD, AETOXGR
L1367: ## Adverse Events
L1368: Treatment-emergent hyperphosphatemia was reported for 134 subjects (81.7%) in the 8/9 mg daily regimen, with a median time to onset of 15 days (range 2 to 116). The maximum severity
L1369: was Grade 1 or 2 for all but 2 subjects with Grade 3 events. Of 134 subjects in the 8/9-mg daily regimen with treatment-emergent hyperphosphatemia, 53 had dose interruptions, 16 had dose reductions, and 1 discontinued erdafitinib due to the event. Both Grade 3 events resolved with dose reductions, one in 8 days and the other in 22 days.
L1370: For all dose regimens combined, 280 subjects (67.9%) experienced TEAEs of hyperphosphatemia: 277 (67.2%) Grade 1-2, and 3 (0.7%) Grade 3. At the time of data cutoff, 253 (90.3%) hyperphosphatemia TEAEs had resolved.

L1374: ## Sequelae
L1375: Clinical consequences of acute, severe hyperphosphatemia are due to the formation of calcium phosphate precipitates and to the resulting hypocalcemia. These can include, tetany, seizures, renal failure due to nephrocalcinosis, and heart block due to cardiac calcifications. Prolonged hyperphosphatemia can also lead to anemia, hypotension, hypercalcemia, renal impairment, hypocalcemia, hyperparathyroidism. The overall incidence of these TEAEs in patients with prolonged hyperphosphatemia (\ge 5.5 \text{ mg/dL} for &gt; 1 \text{ month}) was similar to that in the overall safety database (32.1% vs. 33.5% for the 8-9 mg regimen, and 34.0% vs. 32.9% for all doses combined).

L1393: *included in the warnings and precautions section of the package insert. However, this signal will need to be assessed in the ongoing randomized trial.*
L1394: ## 8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1395: The symptomatic measurement questionnaire was an abbreviated set of self-reported questions rating the subject's experience of pain, fatigue, cough, and dyspnea. The questionnaire was implemented with Amendment 3 to the protocol and was developed by reviewing clinical reports from the subjects in BLC2001 treated prior to Amendment 3. The questionnaire was administered only to subjects in the 8-mg regimen who could read English.
L1396: Subjects were to rate their symptoms on a scale ranging from 0 to 7, with higher scores indicating more severe symptoms. The questionnaire time recall period was 1 week (the past week) and was to be completed at screening, Day 14 of Cycle 1 and Day 1 of Cycles 2 and 3.
L1397: **Reviewer's comment:** *The collection of patient-reported outcomes for this NDA was too rudimentary to allow an in-depth analysis*
L1398: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1399: In the 8-9 mg daily regimen and in all regimens combined, approximately 83% of subjects were ≥ 65 years of age and approximately 14% were age ≥ 75 years. For both patient pools and age cutoffs, overall incidences were within 5% for all TEAEs, serious TEAEs, TEAEs leading to discontinuation, and TEAEs leading to death. Grade 3-4 TEAEs were reported more frequently for subjects ≥ 75 years of age than for those age &lt; 75.
L1400: Approximately 60% of subjects in the 8-9 mg daily regimen and in all regimens combined were male. Overall incidences of TEAEs, serious TEAEs, Grade 3-4 TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to discontinuation were slightly higher among males compared to females.
L1401: **Reviewer's comment:** *Of the 416 patients treated with BALVERSA in clinical studies, 45% were 65 years of age or older, and 12% were 75 years of age or older. No overall significant differences in safety or effectiveness were observed between these patients and younger patients.*

L1405: ## 8.2.8. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1406: There were no special safety studies or clinical trials in this application.
L1407: ## 8.2.9. **Additional Safety Explorations**
L1408: ## **Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development**
L1409: Given the intended clinical use in patients with advanced cancer, no stand-alone fertility or carcinogenicity studies were conducted.
L1410: ## **Human Reproduction and Pregnancy**
L1411: There are no data on the use of erdafitinib in pregnant women. Based on findings in animal reproduction studies, erdafitinib can cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. In a rat embryo-fetal toxicity study, erdafitinib was embryotoxic and teratogenic at exposures less than human exposures at all doses studied.
L1412: Women of childbearing potential are advised to use highly effective contraception prior to and during treatment, and for 3 months after the last dose of erdafitinib. Men are advised to use a condom and not to donate or store semen during treatment and for 3 months after the last dose of erdafitinib.
L1413: There are no data on the presence of erdafitinib in human milk, or the effects of erdafitinib on the breastfed infant, or on milk production. Women are advised not to breastfeed during treatment with erdafitinib and for 3 months following the last dose of erdafitinib.
L1414: ## **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
L1415: Given the intended clinical use in patients with advanced cancer, no stand-alone toxicology studies were conducted in juvenile animals.
L1416: On May 8, 2018, the FDA issued an agreement to the Applicant's initial Pediatric Study Plan, where a full waiver was requested for the indication of urothelial cancer, because studies would be impossible or highly impracticable, and erdafitinib is not likely to be used in a substantial number of pediatric patients.
L1417: On December 12, 2018, the Oncology Subcommittee of the Pediatric Review Committee concurred with the Division's recommendation to grant a full waiver for pediatric studies of erdafitinib for patients with urothelial cancer due to the rarity of this indication in the pediatric population.
L1418: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound**
L1419: There is no information on overdose of erdafitinib and no known specific antidote for erdafitinib overdose. In the event of an overdose, erdafitinib should be stopped and general supportive measures undertaken until clinical toxicity has diminished or resolved.
L1420: ## 8.2.10. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1421: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1422: Erdafitinib is not marketed in any country.
L1423: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1424: Erdafitinib is being approved under the provisions in 21 CFR Part 601 Subpart E (accelerated approval). A confirmatory trial(s) is therefore required to verify and describe the clinical benefit of erdafitinib in patients with urothelial carcinoma. The confirmatory randomized trial BLC3001 is ongoing (see Section 13 of this review).
L1425: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1426: The erdafitinib clinical safety database contains pooled data from a total of 416 patients in two phase 1 dose escalation trials in subjects with advanced solid tumors (EDI1001, and GAC1001) and one phase 2 trial (BLC2001) in patients with advanced or metastatic urothelial cancers. The proposed dose is 8 mg daily with pharmacodynamically guided up-titration to 9 mg daily. A total of 99 subjects with urothelial cancer were treated with this dosing regimen in BLC2001. This includes the 87 patients who form the primary efficacy population for this NDA, as well as 12 patients who were treated in the first-line metastatic setting due to their cisplatin-ineligible status. Additionally, to expand the safety database of this dosing regimen, data from subjects in EDI1001 treated with 9-mg daily (no up-titration; n = 65) was also reviewed, for a total of 164 subjects treated with what is referred to as the 8/9 mg daily regimen.
L1427: Key safety results for this pooled analysis from these 87 patients, 164 patients, and from all 416 patients across the erdafitinib safety database (all doses) are as follows:
L1428: - Commonly reported TEAEs with the 8-9 mg daily regimen, irrespective of attribution, were hyperphosphatemia (81.7%), stomatitis (56.1%), dry mouth (45.7%), diarrhea (41.5%), decreased appetite (37.8%), constipation and paronychia (35.4% each), and dry skin (31.7%). Labeled TEAEs, including laboratory abnormalities, for the 87-patient efficacy population included hyperphosphatemia (75.9%), stomatitis (56.3%), fatigue (54.0%), diarrhea (47.1%), dry mouth (44.8%), and onycholysis (41.4%).
- Grade 3 or 4 TEAEs were reported for 66.5% of subjects receiving the 8-9 mg daily regimen, with 10 subjects (6.1%) reporting Grade 4 events. Commonly reported Grade 3 or 4 TEAEs were stomatitis (10.4%), hyponatremia (9.8%), asthenia (6.1%), palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia syndrome (PPES), and anemia (5.5% each). In the 87-patient efficacy population, Grade 3 or 4 TEAEs were reported for 58.6% of subjects, including stomatitis (10.3%), onycholysis (10.3%), stomatitis (9.2%), and fatigue (8.0%).
L1429: - Serious TEAEs occurred in 41.4% of subjects in the 87-patient efficacy population, 40.9% of subjects on the 8-9 mg daily regimen and 43.3% of subjects on all doses combined. Serious TEAEs reported by 2% or more subjects were general physical health deterioration, dyspnea, urinary tract infection, and abdominal pain.
- Eleven subjects (12.6%) in the 87-patient efficacy population had TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation. Sixteen (11.2%) on the 8-9 mg daily regimen and 36 subjects (9.2%) across all doses had TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation, the most common being general physical health deterioration.
- TEAEs leading to death were reported for 11 subjects (2.6%) across all doses. Except for myocardial infarction in one subject with a prior cardiac history, intracranial hemorrhage in one subject with brain metastases, and sepsis in one subject with head and neck carcinoma, all TEAEs leading to death occurred in the context of progressive disease.
L1430: Expected toxicities of FGFR inhibitors as a class include eye disorders, nail and skin disorders, and hyperphosphatemia. In addition, arrhythmia-related TEAEs were considered adverse events of interest because of preclinical findings. These toxicities are presented in the pooled 8/9 mg daily regimen and overall safety database.
L1431: ## - Eye disorders
L1432: TEAEs consistent with central serous retinopathy (CSR) were reported in 26 (15.9%) of subjects on the 8/9-mg daily regimen. Grade 3 events consistent with CSR were retinopathy, detachment of retinal pigment epithelium, and retinal edema in one subject each. Three subjects permanently discontinued treatment due to such events (detachment of retinal pigment epithelium [n = 2], and choriorretinopathy [n=1]). At the time of data cutoff, 14 central serous retinopathy events had resolved, while 12 events were ongoing, all Grade 1-2.
L1433: Other eye toxicities such as dry eye or keratitis were observed for 44.5% of subjects on the 8/9 mg daily regimen and 37.5% of subjects across all dose levels. Approximately 4% of these events led to discontinuation of study treatment.
L1434: ## - Nail and skin disorders
L1435: Nail disorders, most commonly onycholysis, paronychia, and nail dystrophy, were reported in 53.0% of subjects on the 8-9 mg daily regimen and in 41.3% of subjects across all doses. Skin disorders, most commonly dry skin and PPES, were reported in 47.6% of subjects on the 8-9 mg daily regimen and 42.8% of subjects across all doses. Two subjects discontinued treatment due to nail disorders and 4 subjects discontinued due to skin disorders.
L1436: ## - Hyperphosphatemia
L1437: Treatment-emergent hyperphosphatemia, thought to result from inhibition by erdafitinib of renal FGF23 signaling, was reported in 81.7% of subjects on the 8-9 mg daily regimen and in 67.3% across all dose regimens. Mean phosphate values peaked at approximately 6 mg/dL approximately 6 weeks after the start of treatment and subsequently decreased to less than 4.5 mg/dL by Cycle 6 (due to dose reduction of erdafitinib, the use of phosphate binders, or other unidentified mechanisms). Hyperphosphatemia was added to section 5 of the package insert because of the frequency and expectedness of this laboratory abnormality in patients being treated with erdafitinib.
L1438: ## - Arrhythmias
L1439: Because preclinical studies identified arrhythmia as a potentially important adverse event, electrocardiography and echocardiography were monitored in the phase 1 trials (predose, and 2, 4, and 8 hours postdose on Days 1, 8 and 15 of Cycles 1 and on Day 1 of Cycle 2; and postdose on subsequent cycles). No adverse effect of erdafitinib on QT interval or cardiac function were observed, and the incidence of arrhythmia-related TEAEs across all dosing regimens was low (3.6%). In addition, an analysis of time-matched QTc and Holter data from the phase 1 trial EDI1001 detected no effect of erdafitinib on cardiac repolarization. However, in one patient (Subject [REDACTED](b) (6)) with no stated history of cardiac disease experienced an arrhythmia soon after starting erdafitinib. Arrhythmias should therefore be assessed in the ongoing randomized controlled trial.
L1440: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1441: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1442: In general, there were no notable statistical issues with the study design, statistical analysis plan, or efficacy results for the BLC2001 study. The study showed a confirmed ORR of 32.2% (95% CI: 22.4, 42.0) in the target chemotherapy-relapsed/refractory population (N=87) with an estimated median duration of response of 5.42 months (95% CI: 4.21, 6.93) per BIRC assessment. The study showed a confirmed ORR of 32.2% (95% CI: 22.4, 42.0) in the target chemotherapy-relapsed/refractory population at the proposed dose (N=87) with an estimated median duration of response of 5.42 months (95% CI: 4.21, 6.93) per BIRC assessment. Efficacy results based on investigator were consistent with BIRC assessment. We reiterate that no inferential procedures can be used to evaluate results from this single arm study. Instead, the efficacy evaluation was based on the magnitude of response rate and adequate duration of response. Additionally, although progression-free survival, time to response and overall survival results were summarized, we noted that time-to-event endpoints are uninterpretable without a comparator arm. The real world data submitted in the application to augment the clinical trial data did not provide additional supportive evidence.
L1443: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1444: The review team recommends accelerated approval for erdafitinib 8 mg (two 4 mg tablets) orally once daily, with dose increase to 9 mg (three 3 mg tablets) once daily based on serum phosphate levels and tolerability at 14 to 21 days. Erdafitinib is indicated for the treatment of adult patients with locally advanced or metastatic urothelial carcinoma that has:
L1445: - susceptible FGFR3 or FGFR2 genetic alterations, and
- progressed during or following at least one line of prior platinum-containing chemotherapy, including within 12 months of neoadjuvant or adjuvant platinum-containing chemotherapy.
L1446: Patients should be selected for therapy based on an FDA-approved companion diagnostic.

Use in Specific Populations (Pregnancy 8.1 – Animal Data)	(b) (4) FDA revised and added additional detail to clarify the nonclinical findings in the embryo-fetal study:
......Doses \ge 4\text{mg/kg/day} produced embryo-fetal death, major blood vessel malformations and other vascular anomalies, limb malformations (ectrodactyly, absent or misshapen long bones), an increased incidence of skeletal anomalies in multiple bones (vertebrae, sternebrae, ribs), and decreased fetal weight."
Use in Specific Populations (Lactation 8.2, Risk Summary)	(b) (4) FDA revised this section to reflect data from section 12.3 Pharmacokinetics, Elimination. No milk data is available in humans or animals. Effect on rat mammary gland (atrophy) was reported in one month and 13-week repeat dose toxicology studies, however recovery was
L1461: reported in the one month study (not measured in 13-week study).
The duration of time a patient should wait to breastfeed following last dose was calculated using the higher limit of erdafitinib half life (T_{1/2}) from clinical studies which was 120.5 hours. Five half lives is considered sufficient to allow full clearance of drug, which amounts to approximately 30 days. P/T provides the following recommendation:
"......, advise lactating women not to breastfeed during treatment with BALVERSA and for one month following the last dose"

Section 8.3 - Fertility	(b) (4)

"Based on animal studies, BALVERSA may impair fertility in females of reproductive potential"

Section 8.4 - Pediatric Use	Safety and effectiveness of BALVERSA in pediatric patients have not been established.	
FDA revised this section to reflect data from the 13-week studies in rats and dogs:
"Safety and effectiveness of BALVERSA in pediatric patients have not been established. In 13-week repeat-dose toxicology studies in rats and dogs, toxicities in bone and teeth were observed at an exposure less than the human exposure (AUC) at the maximum recommended human dose. Chondroid dysplasia/metaplasia were reported in multiple bones in both species, and tooth abnormalities (including discoloration/malocclusion and degeneration of odontoblasts) were reported in rats."

Section 12 - Clinical Pharmacology (Mechanism of Action 12.1)	(b) (4)	
FDA revised this section to reflect data from the pharmacology in vitro and in vivo studies:
"Erdafitinib is a kinase inhibitor that binds to FGFR1, FGFR2, FGFR3 and FGFR4 and inhibits enzymatic activity based on in vitro data. Erdafitinib also binds to RET, CSF1R, PDGFRA, PDGFRB, FLT4, KIT, and VEGFR2. Erdafitinib inhibited FGFR phosphorylation and signaling and decreased cell viability in cell lines expressing FGFR genetic alterations, including mutations, amplifications, and fusions. Erdafitinib demonstrated antitumor activity in FGFR-expressing cell lines and xenograft models, derived from tumor types, including bladder cancer."

L1466: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L1467: ## No REMS was required based on review of the safety results of this application
L1468: ## 12.1. Central Serous Retinopathy (CSR)
L1469: The clinical trials that generated the safety population for this application excluded from enrollment patients with the following corneal or retinal conditions thought to increase the risk of eye toxicity (the ongoing randomized trial BLC3001 has these exclusion criteria as well):
L1470: - History of CSR or retinal vascular occlusion
- Active wet, age-related macular degeneration
- Nonproliferative diabetic retinopathy with macular edema
- Uncontrolled glaucoma
- Corneal pathology such as keratitis, keratoconjunctivitis, keratopathy, corneal abrasion, inflammation or ulceration.
L1471: The Applicant proposed the following risk mitigation measures for ocular toxicity:
L1472: - Baseline ophthalmologic examination to include evaluation of visual acuity, tonometry, slit lamp examination including screening for dry eye, fundoscopy, Amsler grid test, and OCT if indicated by findings on fundoscopy. Should signs of active, ≥ Grade 2 CSR be found, treatment should be delayed until resolution.
- Monthly Amsler Grid testing and holding erdafitinib if evidence of early CSR is detected. When CSR is suspected, OCT should be performed.
- Use of hydrating and lubricating eye gels or ointments every two hours while awake, beginning the start of treatment and continue during the entire treatment period.
L1473: Reviewer's Recommendation:
L1474: - A history of prior macular or retinal disease (e.g., CSR, RVO, age related macular degeneration, diabetic neuropathy) without active RPED/CSR did not predict the occurrence of CSR on EDI1001 or BLC2001. Therefore, this need not be retained as a contraindication for erdafitinib use.
- Sections 2.4 and 5.1 of the Prescribing Information should recommend an ophthalmological examination at baseline and monthly during treatment, and provide dose modification guidelines for patients developing eye disorders.
- Section 17 of the Prescribing Information should provide advice to patients developing eye problems.
- Ophthalmological examinations should include fundoscopy, slit lamp observation, and OCT. Amsler grid testing is insensitive for CSR, and should not be recommended. Routine tonometry should not be recommended.
- Dry eye symptoms are frequent during treatment with erdafitinib. All patients should receive prophylaxis with ocular demulcents as needed; however, recommendations to
L1475: *use lubricating eye gels are discouraged since there are no FDA-approved dry eye therapy gels.*
L1476: - A REMS is not required to address ocular toxicity
L1477: ## 12.2. Teratogenicity
L1478: Based on findings in animal reproduction studies, erdafitinib can cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. In a rat embryo-fetal toxicity study, erdafitinib was embryotoxic and teratogenic at exposures less than the human exposures at all doses studied. Fetal toxicity was characterized by hand/foot defects and malformations of major blood vessels. No teratogenicity events with the use of erdafitinib have been reported from the available clinical trials. There are no clinical trials of erdafitinib in pregnant women.
L1479: Sections 5.2, 8.1, and 17 of the Prescribing Information will contain advice for pregnant women, females of reproductive potential, and male patients.
L1480: ## 12.3. Drug-Drug Interaction (P-glycoprotein inhibition)
L1481: Erdafitinib inhibited human P-gp in vitro. Physiologically-based PK simulation predicted a Cmax-ratio of 1.45 and an AUC-ratio of 1.18 for digoxin when erdafitinib was coadministered with digoxin at the same time, whereas dose staggering by 6 hours could avoid this interaction.
L1482: Sections 7.2 and 12.3 of the Prescribing Information will contain recommendations regarding the use of erdafitinib with narrow therapeutic index P-gp substrates, and Section 17 will contain information for patients using concomitant medications.
L1483: ## 12.4. Use During Pregnancy and Lactation
L1484: Sections 8.1, 8.2, and 17 will provide advice for pregnant women, female patients of reproductive potential, and women who are breastfeeding or plan to breastfeed.
L1485: ## 12.5. Use in Patients with Moderate to Severe Hepatic Failure
L1486: In the safety database, 25 patients with mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN, or ULN &lt; total bilirubin \le 1.5 x ULN) at baseline were exposed to erdafitinib for 142.5 person-months. Based on population PK analysis, there were no clinically meaningful differences in exposure between subjects with mild hepatic impairment and subjects of normal hepatic function. No data are available in UC subjects with moderate (1.5 x ULN &lt; total bilirubin \le 3x ULN) or severe (total bilirubin &gt; 3 x ULN) hepatic impairment at baseline. A Phase 1 trial (EDI1008) to evaluate the effect of hepatic impairment on the PK of erdafitinib is planned.
L1487: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1488: - PMR 3561-1. Submit the final report and datasets demonstrating clinical benefit of erdafitinib in patients with locally advanced and metastatic urothelial carcinoma with susceptible FGFR3 or 2 genetic alterations from clinical trial BLC3001: "A Phase 3 Study of Erdafitinib Compared With Vinflunine or Docetaxel or Pembrolizumab in Subjects with Advanced Urothelial Cancer and Selected FGFR Gene Aberrations." PMR 3561-1. Submit the final report and datasets demonstrating clinical benefit of erdafitinib in patients with locally advanced and metastatic urothelial carcinoma with susceptible FGFR 3 or 2 genetic alterations from clinical trial BLC3001: "A Phase 3 Study of Erdafitinib Compared With Vinflunine or Docetaxel or Pembrolizumab in Subjects with Advanced Urothelial Cancer and Selected FGFR Gene Aberrations."
- PMR 3561-2. Complete and submit the results of your ongoing hepatic impairment study EDI1008 titled "A Phase 1, Open-Label, Single-Dose Study to Evaluate the Effect of Hepatic Impairment on the Pharmacokinetics of Erdafitinib".
- PMR 3561-3. Submit the datasets with the final report of a clinical pharmacokinetic trial that evaluates the effect of repeated doses of erdafitinib on the single dose pharmacokinetics of a sensitive CYP3A substrate (e.g., midazolam), to address the potential for excessive drug toxicity or decreased drug exposure, and to determine appropriate dosing recommendations when coadministering erdafitinib with a sensitive CYP3A substrate. This trial should be designed and conducted in accordance with FDA Draft Guidance for Industry: Clinical Drug Interaction Studies—Study Design, Data Analysis, and Clinical Implications.
- PMC 3561-4. Submit the datasets with the final report from a clinical pharmacokinetic trial that evaluates the effect of repeated doses of a strong inducer (e.g., rifampin) of CYP2C9 and CYP3A on the single dose pharmacokinetics of erdafitinib to assess the magnitude of decreased drug exposure and to determine appropriate dosing recommendations when erdafitinib is coadministered with CYP2C9 and CYP3A inducers. This trial should be designed and conducted in accordance with the FDA Draft Guidance for Industry: "Clinical Drug Interaction Studies—Study Design, Data Analysis, and Clinical Implications."
- PMC 3561-5. Submit the datasets with the final report from a clinical pharmacokinetic study that evaluates the effect of repeated doses of erdafitinib (at steady-state) on the single dose pharmacokinetics of a probe substrate of OCT2 to determine appropriate dosing recommendations for OCT2 substrate when it is coadministered with erdafitinib. This trial should be designed and conducted in accordance with the FDA Draft Guidance for Industry: "Clinical Drug Interaction Studies—Study Design, Data Analysis, and Clinical Implications."

L1537: ## 19.4.3. Population PK Analyses
L1538: The Applicant submitted a population PKPD report entitled "Population Pharmacokinetic Pharmacodynamic Report for Erdafitinib (b)(4) to characterize the PK of erdafitinib in health subjects and patients with cancer and the time course of serum phosphate concentrations in patients
L1539: **Objectives:** The general aim of the population PK and PK-PD analyses was to characterize the PK of total and free erdafitinib plasma concentrations after various oral dose levels and dosing regimens of erdafitinib, and to subsequently link erdafitinib drug concentrations to the time course of serum phosphate concentrations.
L1540: The specific objectives of these analyses were:
L1541: - To characterize the population PK of erdafitinib including typical PK parameters and quantify inter- and intra-individual variability.
L1542: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation (NDA [NME] 212018)
## BALVERSA™ (erdafitinib)
L1543: - To provide a quantitative assessment of the effect of intrinsic and extrinsic factors on erdafitinib exposure.
- To characterize the PK-PD parameters characterizing the effect of erdafitinib total and free plasma concentrations on serum phosphate concentrations and quantify their inter- and intra-individual variability.
- To provide a quantitative assessment of the potential effect of the intrinsic and extrinsic factors on erdafitinib-related phosphate changes
L1544: **Data:** Two datasets, a PK dataset and a PK-PD dataset, were used for the PK and PK-PD analyses, respectively. A total of 6 clinical studies (EDI1001, EDI1002, EDI1003, EDI1005, BLC2001, and GAC1001) were included in the pooled dataset for population PK analysis, while 3 clinical studies conducted in subjects with cancer (EDI1001, BLC2001, and GAC1001) were selected for PK-PD analysis.
L1545: The final PK analysis included data from 373 subjects receiving oral single doses (range: 10 to 12 mg), continuous once daily doses (range: 0.5 to 12 mg), or intermittent once daily doses (range: 10 to 12 mg) of erdafitinib, and included 5060 total erdafitinib plasma concentrations and 650 free erdafitinib plasma concentrations. Summary statistics of the continuous and categorical subject covariates most relevant for the PK analysis are presented in **Table 126**.

L1559: ## Population PK Model Development
L1560: **Final Model:** The final population PK model describing the time profiles of erdafitinib total and free plasma concentrations was an open, linear, 3-compartment disposition model with first-order absorption and elimination. A lag-time (t_{lag}) was used to describe the plasma PK profile of total and free erdafitinib. The binding of erdafitinib to plasma proteins was linear with respect to erdafitinib concentration. Free erdafitinib plasma concentrations were derived from total concentrations using measured FU. The model was parameterized in terms of the apparent central volume of distribution of total drug (V_{2,tot/F}), 2 apparent peripheral volumes of distribution (V_{3/F} and V_{4/F}), the apparent clearance of free erdafitinib (CL_{free/F}), and the apparent inter-compartmental clearances of free (Q_{3,free/F} and Q_{4,free/F}). Inter-individual variability (IIV) in model parameters was quantified using an exponential model for CL_{free/F}, V_{2,tot/F}, V_{3/F}, and apparent first-order absorption rate constant (k_a). Residual unexplained variability (RUV) was additive on natural logarithmic-transformed plasma concentrations and was estimated separately for total and free erdafitinib concentrations. A schematic representation of the final population PK model for erdafitinib is demonstrated in **Figure 9**. The parameter estimates for the final model including covariate effects are summarized in **Table 127**.

L1582: **Effect of Covariates on Erdafitinib PK:** Within the range of covariate values evaluated as part of the full model, the impact of age, race/ethnicity (Hispanic, Asian), and mild hepatic impairment on erdafitinib exposure was not statistically significant (Figure 12). In the final model, sex and mild and moderate renal impairment had a statistically significant effect on CL_{free}/F, while weight, sex, and AGP were statistically significant on V_{2tot}/F. Once AGP was incorporated as a covariate on V_{2tot}/F, the effect of health status on V_{2tot}/F was no longer statistically significant. On average, women had 18.8% lower CL_{free}/F relative to men, and subjects with mild and moderate renal impairment had a 21.1% and 21.9% higher CL_{free}/F relative to subjects with normal renal function. On average, subjects with low (&lt;60 kg) and high (&gt;80 kg) body weight had 9.82% lower and 19.8% higher V_{2tot}/F, respectively, relative to median weight (60 to 80 kg). A 50% decrease in AGP (as observed between healthy subjects and subjects with cancer) was estimated to lead to a 38.8% increase in V_{2tot}/F and a 50% increase in CL/F, but no effect on CL_{free}/F.

L1591: ## Population PK-PD Analysis Development
L1592: **Data:** Three clinical studies (EDI1001, BLC2001, and GAC1001) conducted in subjects diagnosed with cancer were included in the pooled dataset for population PK-PD analysis. Data from 345
L1593: subjects receiving continuous once daily doses (range: 0.5 to 12 mg) or intermittent once daily doses (range: 10 to 12 mg) of erdafitinib, contributing 4639 total serum phosphate concentrations, were included in the analysis. Subject characteristics were similar to those described above for the PK analysis dataset. The exploration of the prognostic factors in the analysis dataset evidenced that for subjects with fibroblast growth factor receptor (FGFR)-positive tumors, 49.6% (N = 123) had FGFR mutations, 20.6% (N = 51) had FGFR alterations, and 29.8% (N = 74) had FGFR translocations. Urothelial cancer was diagnosed in 44.3% (N = 153) of subjects, whereas 55.7% (N = 192) subjects were diagnosed with other types of cancer. A total of 116 (33.6%), 192 (55.7%), and 18 (5.2%) subjects were classified in the Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) status grade 0, 1, or 2, respectively. Visceral metastases were present in 57.7% (N = 199) of subjects. A phosphate binder was coadministered at least once for 165 (47.8%) subjects. Most of the subjects were chemotherapy-relapsed/refractory (91%, N = 314), whereas 3.5% (N = 12) were chemotherapy-naïve. The mean (range) baseline hemoglobin was 11.8 g/dL (7.90 to 16.7 g/dL).
L1594: ## Population PKPD Model Development
L1595: The relationship between the absolute change from baseline in serum phosphate concentrations ([PO4]) and free erdafitinib plasma concentrations in a hypothetical biophase (or effect compartment) was characterized by a slope model, with a coefficient (M) and an exponent (y). In addition, the equilibration rate constant to predict erdafitinib free concentrations at biophase (effect compartment rate constant [ke0]) and the serum phosphate concentrations at pre-treatment baseline ([PO4]BSL) were also estimated from the data according to the following equation:
L1596: [\text{PO}_4]_t = [\text{PO}_4]_{\text{BSL}} + M \cdot C_e^y, \text{ where } \frac{dC_e}{dt} = ke0 \cdot (C_{\text{free}} - C_e)
L1597: Phosphate data pointed toward an attenuation of the drug effect on phosphate over time. This effect was empirically modelled by a time-dependent function of M:
L1598: M = m \cdot (1 - T), \text{ where } \frac{dT}{dt} = k_{\text{in}} \cdot (1 - T)
L1599: where T was the amount of attenuation over time, k_{\text{in}} was the rate constant describing the attenuation of drug effect with time, and m was the coefficient of the slope model describing the relationship between [PO4] and C_e at time 0. In addition, treatment interruptions longer than approximately 24 days and treatment discontinuation resulted in a new but lower plateau phosphate concentration as compared with pre-treatment baseline. This was accounted for using an empirical time-dependent function of [PO4]BSL:
L1600: [\text{PO}_4]_{\text{BSL}} = [\text{PO}_4]_0 \quad \text{if } (T_{\text{SLD}} \le t_{\text{lag}})
L1601: [\text{PO}_4]_{\text{BSL}} = [\text{PO}_4]_p - ([\text{PO}_4]_p - [\text{PO}_4]_0) \cdot e^{(-k_{\text{base}} \cdot (T_{\text{SLD}} - t_{\text{lag}}))} \quad \text{if } (T_{\text{SLD}} &gt; t_{\text{lag}})
L1602: With [PO4] as the population estimate of the phosphate baseline value when TSLD was less or
L1603: equal to tlag (pre-treatment baseline), TSLD as the time since last dose, [PO4]P as the estimate of the phosphate baseline value plateau, kbase as the rate of decline of phosphate baseline value with time, and tlag as the time delay after which phosphate baseline value started to decline with time.
L1604: IIV in model parameters was quantified using an additive model for [PO4]0 and [PO4]P and an exponential model for m, ke0, and kin. RUV was additive on natural logarithmic-transformed serum phosphate concentrations.
L1605: The parameter estimates of the final PK-PD model are presented in **Table 130**. All parameters were estimated with adequate precision, as measured by a RSE below 14.8% for fixed effects and below 15.8% for random effects. The goodness-of-fit plots for the final PKPD model is shown in Figure 13. The VPC plots for the final PKPD model is shown in Figure 14.
L1606: **Table 130: Population Parameter Estimates for the Final Population PK-PD Model**
L1607: Parameters	Description	Final Model Estimate (RSE%)
[PO4]0 (mg/dL)	Phosphate baseline	3.08 (1.4%)
m (Slope) (mg/dL/(ng/mL))	Slope of drug effect	0.869 (5.5%)
\gamma (Power exponent)	Exponent of drug effect relationship	0.860 (4.7%)
ke0 (h-1)	Effect compartment rate constant	0.0199 (2.9%)
kin (h-1)	Rate constant describing attenuation of drug effect over time	0.000102 (1.5%)
\theta_{SEX} (%)	Gender effect on phosphate baseline (proportional change for females)	12.4 (14.8%)

L1619: **Serum Phosphate Concentration-Time Course:** The relationship between serum phosphate concentrations and erdafitinib free concentrations at biophase was characterized by a slope (M) and an exponent (y), with the equilibration rate constant accounting for the delay between PK and PD effect. Near steady state for free concentrations in the biophase were reached
L1620: around the same time as the steady state for free concentrations in plasma. Figure 15 represents the time course of erdafitinib free concentrations in plasma and in biophase, as well as the increase in phosphate with repeated daily dosing. The relationship between serum phosphate and free erdafitinib concentration in the biophase was almost linear with an exponent of 0.86, with approximately 0.48 mg/dL increase in phosphate concentrations for an increase in erdafitinib free plasma concentration from 0 to 0.5 ng/mL at the first dose. According to this model, doubling the free concentration resulted in a 1.8-fold increase in drug-related phosphate changes. Serum phosphate concentrations increased after administration of the first dose and reached more than 95% maximal level after about 14 days of starting erdafitinib treatment (Figure 15).
L1621: **Figure 15: Simulated Free Erdafitinib Plasma Concentrations, Free Erdafitinib Concentrations at Biophase and Serum Phosphate Concentrations for a Typical Subject with Urothelial Carcinoma, Based on the Final PK-PD Model (8 mg Once Daily for 1 Month)**
L1622: Source: Figure 13 on page 68 of Applicant's population PKPD report
L1623: The slope of the drug effect declined with time, with a reduction of 50% in the slope of the drug effect predicted after 9.5 months. This effect was only time-dependent and not concentration related. This translated into a decrease in serum phosphate concentrations with time under a constant dosing regimen and non-stationary serum phosphate concentrations. Based on the typical value of model parameters, serum phosphate concentrations reached a level of about 5.6 mg/dL after 14 days of dosing with erdafitinib 8 mg dose and started declining steadily after 1 month of treatment, resulting in lower phosphate concentration of approximately 1 mg/dL over 9 months (from the 2nd to the 10th months after treatment initiation) despite continuous
L1624: dosing (Figure 16). In addition, upon treatment interruption or discontinuation, phosphate concentrations declined with time to reach a level lower than the pre-treatment baseline: approximately 580 hours (24 days) after the last dose of erdafitinib, a post-treatment baseline phosphate concentration was re-established at 2.67 mg/dL, lower than the pre-treatment baseline of 3.08 mg/dL
L1625: **Figure 16: Simulated Serum Phosphate Concentration-Time Profile for a Typical Subject With Urothelial Carcinoma, Based on the Final PK-PD Model (8 mg Once Daily for 9 Months)**
L1626: *Source: Figure 15 on page 69 of Applicant's population PKPD report*
L1627: *Reviewer's comments: The population PK-PD analysis is acceptable. The final population PKPD model was able to characterize the serum phosphate concentrations as indicated in the applicant's diagnostic plots. The reviewer agrees that the proposed dosing regimen is acceptable based on the PK/PD simulation results,*
L1628: **Simulations of Proposed Dosing Regimen:** The population PK (full covariate model) and PK/PD models linking erdafitinib dose to serum phosphate (PO4) concentrations were used to simulate PK and serum phosphate concentrations in fibroblast growth factor receptor positive (FGFR+) urothelial cancer subjects. A virtual population of 1000 or 10,000 (for the covariate effects scenarios) random virtual subjects were sampled from the final PK/PD model parameter distributions.
L1629: For the proposed pharmacodynamically guided dosing algorithm scenario, phosphate concentrations over time are summarized in Figure 17. The proportion of subjects with phosphate concentrations in different concentration ranges is shown in Figure 18. The proportion of subjects given different doses over time is shown in Figure 19.

## BALVERSATM (erdafitinib)
L1631: As shown Figure 17, the proposed pharmacodynamically guided dosing algorithm was predicted to result in 29% of subjects within the phosphate target concentrations range after both 1 and 4 cycles (C1 and C4) of treatment. At the end of cycle 4 (C4), the median treatment duration in the current dataset of BLC2001, 65% of subjects remained below this range (phosphate concentrations &lt;5.5 mg/dL), while only very few subjects were above the range (5% and &lt;1\% within 7.0–9.0 mg/dL and above 9.0 mg/dL, respectively). Around 38% of subjects had their treatment interrupted at least once.

L1640: Reviewer's Comments: There were limitations to the above analysis. All simulations and dose adjustments were only based on serum phosphate concentrations, whereas in BLC2001 individualized dose up-titrations, interruptions and reductions were also triggered by other safety endpoints as recommended in the study protocol. No model was available to account for the occurrence of such events. Therefore, predicted proportions may not provide a fully accurate estimation of the different endpoints such as proportions of subjects at different phosphate and dose levels compared to data observed in BLC2001
L1641: Despite the limitation, the simulations generally support that the proposed starting dose of 8 mg with individualized up-titration to 9 mg on day 14 if serum phosphate concentration is below 5.5 mg/dL which maximizes the number of subjects with serum phosphate concentrations within the target range defined in BLC2001 while minimizing treatment interruptions and dose reductions.
L1642: ## 19.4.4. PD-Clinical Endpoints Analyses
L1643: ## Relationship between PO4 and Efficacy/Safety Endpoints
L1644: The applicant submitted a separate exposure-response (ER) analysis report entitled "Exposure Response (Efficacy and Safety) Report Protocol 42756493BLC2001; Phase 2" based on data from study BLC2001. The ER analyses were able to describe the relationship between serum phosphate and objective response rate (ORR) as well as relationship between serum phosphate and selected clinical safety endpoints (eye disorders, nail disorders, CSR, palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia syndrome (PPES), and skin disorders.
L1645: **Objective:** The objectives of this report were:
L1646: - To characterize the relationship between exposure to serum phosphate and objective response rate based on best response (ORR, comprising complete response [CR] and partial response [PR]), the primary efficacy endpoint of Study BLC2001. In addition, secondary objectives were to explore the relationship between exposure to serum phosphate and disease control rate (DCR, comprising CR, PR, and stable disease) and progression-free survival (PFS).
- To characterize the relationship between exposure to erdafitinib and/or serum phosphate and selected clinical safety endpoints: eye disorders (excluding central serous retinopathy [CSR]), CSR, nail disorders, palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia (PPES), and skin disorders.
L1647: **Data:** The efficacy and safety data were from Phase 2 study BLC2001. Study BLC2001 is a Phase 2, multicenter, open-label study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of single-agent erdafitinib in subjects with locally advanced or metastatic urothelial carcinoma and whose tumors had certain FGFR genetic alterations. Three dosing regimens were involved in the study. At the start of the study, subjects were randomized to either Regimen 1 (10 mg intermittent) or Regimen 2 (6 mg daily). Subjects in Regimen 1 could increase their dose to 12 mg intermittent and subjects in Regimen 2 could increase their dose to 8 mg daily, based on the serum phosphate concentrations on Cycle 1 Day 28 and absence of any drug-related disorder. At interim analysis 1 [IA1], based on efficacy and safety data and supported by preliminary PK-PD modeling, the data review committee decided to terminate further enrollment to the intermittent regimen and selected a higher continuous dose of 8 mg daily (Regimen 3). Subjects in Regimen 3 could increase their dose to 9 mg daily based on the phosphate level on Cycle 1 Day 14 in absence of any drug-related disorder. Overview of data included in the population ER efficacy and safety analysis is demonstrated in Table 132.
L1648: **Table 132: Overview of Data Included in the Population ER Efficacy and Safety Analyses**

L1650: a Details of the PK and phosphate assessments are available in the population PK-PD analysis plan.1
L1651: **Exposure Metrics:** The exposure metrics were derived from population PK and PK-PD models. The PK exposure metrics of erdafitinib used to assess their relationship with safety endpoints:
L1652: - Predicted area under the concentration curve from 0 to 24 hours (AUC_{0-24})
- Predicted trough concentration at predose (C_{min})
- Predicted C_{max}, if the data allowed.
L1653: The metrics were obtained for total and free erdafitinib concentrations. Each metric was derived up to the end of the up-titration period (Day 1 to 14, early exposure metric) and up to the day when the highest severity of the disorder occurred for first time (overall exposure metric).
L1654: **Serum Phosphate as Exposure Metric:** The average daily phosphate concentration was the PD exposure metric obtained for each subject to assess their relationship with efficacy or safety endpoints. This PD metric was derived up to the end of the up-titration period (Day 1 to 14, early exposure metric), up to the day of first response assessment (approximately 6 weeks, overall exposure metric) for the efficacy analysis, or up to the day when the highest severity of the disorder occurred for first time for the safety analysis.
L1655: **Efficacy Endpoints:** The primary endpoint for the study and the ER analysis for efficacy was ORR, which was defined as the proportion of subjects with CR or PR as their best objective response (BOR). Subjects were assessed for disease response every 6 weeks during the first 3 months. Thereafter, scans were performed every 12 weeks for the next 9 months, then every 4 to 6 months until disease progression.
L1656: The DCR and PFS were used as secondary exploratory endpoints in the ER analysis for efficacy. The DCR was defined as the rate of subjects having CR, PR, or stable disease. PFS was defined as the duration from the date of the first dose of study drug until the date of first documented evidence of progressive disease (or relapse for subjects who experience CR during the study) or death, whichever came first, for subjects experiencing progression/death.
L1657: **Safety Endpoints:** The key safety endpoints included in the exposure-safety analyses were selected based on their clinical relevance, occurrence (higher than 10%), or presence of high degree of severity (Grade \ge3). Based on the mentioned criteria, the safety endpoints included in the analysis were the following disorders: eye, CSR, nail, PPES, and skin.
L1658: Summary of demographic and prognostic data used for ER efficacy and safety analysis is provided in Table 133.

L1666: **PO4-Response Analysis for Efficacy:** Univariate and multivariate logistic analyses were explored to characterize the relationship between ORR and serum phosphate concentrations. The final model for investigator's response included average daily phosphate concentration up to 6 weeks and FGFR alterations as predictors (Model D in Figure 21). The phosphate effect was statistically significant (OR: 1.38; 95% CI: 1.02, 1.86; Model D, p=0.04). The probability of response as a function of serum phosphate concentration stratified by prognostic factor categories is displayed in Figure 20 and Figure 21. Based on the analysis, higher erdafitinib doses led to higher serum phosphate concentrations and thus increased probability of response. Specifically, given the median average daily phosphate concentration of 4.99 mg/dL for subjects in the 6 mg regimen and 5.34 mg/dL for subjects in the 8 mg regimen predicted after accounting for dose titration, the model-predicted increase in the response odds was 12% (OR: 1.12; 95% CI: 1.01, 1.24 for 0.35 mg/dL serum phosphate). Assuming fixed doses with no titrations, continuous regimens of 6, 8, and 9 mg daily were predicted to result in median average serum phosphate concentrations of 5.05, 5.61, and 5.89 mg/dL, respectively. Under these assumptions, the model-predicted increases in the response odds were 20% (OR: 1.20;

L1675: Separately, the Applicant performed phosphate-response for efficacy using BIRC response (ORR). L1695: **PO4-Response Analyses for Safety:** The relationship between plasma erdafitinib and/or serum phosphate exposure and the incidence of selected disorders were explored using univariate logistic regression analysis. Within the range of average daily serum phosphate concentrations observed in erdafitinib-treated subjects (2.6 to 10.5 mg/dL), the magnitude of the association varied across the different disorders and was strongest (OR&gt;2) with nail and eye disorders.
