L1702: ## 19.4.5. Exposure-Response Analyses
L1703: **Objective:** The objectives of the exposure-response analyses were:
L1704: - To characterize the relationship between exposure to serum erdafitinib and objective response rate based on best response (ORR, comprising complete response [CR] and partial response [PR]), the primary efficacy endpoint of Study BLC2001.
L1705: - To characterize the relationship between exposure to erdafitinib and selected clinical safety endpoints: eye disorders (excluding central serous retinopathy [CSR]), CSR, nail disorders, palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia (PPES), and skin disorders.
L1706: **Exposure-Response Analyses for Efficacy (ORR):** The primary ER analysis was based on best response as determined by investigator for both Regimen 2 and Regimen 3 (6 and 8 mg continuous doses). The average of the daily area under the free erdafitinib plasma concentration-time curve, AUCfree, and investigator's ORR were correlated based on contingency tables and Pearson's chi-square tests (**Table 138**). No major differences in ORR between the terciles of average daily AUCfree up to Day 14 or 6 week of treatment were observed.

L1729: ## 19.4.6. Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic (PBPK) Analyses
L1730: ## Executive Summary
L1731: The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant's study report (No. FK12149) entitled "A physiological based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) approach to assess potential drug-drug interactions with erdafitinib as victim and as perpetrator" to support the intended uses. Specifically, the Applicant applied the PBPK modeling approach to (1) predict the pharmacokinetics (PK) of erdafitinib in CYP2C9 poor metabolizers; (2) assess the effects of fluconazole (a moderate CYP2C9 and CYP3A inhibitor), itraconazole (a strong CYP3A inhibitor), and rifampin (a strong CYP3A and moderate CYP2C9 inducer) on erdafitinib PK; and (3) assess the effects of erdafitinib on the PK of midazolam and alprazolam (CYP3A4 substrates), metformin (a OCT2 substrate), and digoxin (a P-gp substrate).
L1732: The Division of Pharmacometrics has reviewed the report, supporting modeling files, and the Applicant's responses to our information requests submitted on November 30, 2018, and January 31, 2019 and concluded the following.
L1733: - The erdafitinib PBPK model is adequate to predict the PK in CYP2C9 poor metabolizers. The model predicted that erdafitinib exposure (AUC) could be increased approximately by 60% in subjects with CYP2C9 *3/*3 genotype compared to that in subjects with CYP2C9 *1/*1 genotype.
- The PBPK models are adequate to predict the effects of fluconazole (a moderate CYP2C9 and CYP3A inhibitor), itraconazole (a strong CYP3A inhibitor), and rifampin (a strong CYP3A and moderate CYP2C9 inducer) on erdafitinib PK. The model predicted that a dual CYP2C9 and CYP3A4 inhibitor (such as fluconazole) may increase the erdafitinib exposure by approximately 60% to 100%, and a dual CYP2C9 and CYP3A inducer may decrease the erdafitinib exposure by approximately 30%-60% compared to the exposure in subjects with CYP2C9 *1/*1 without comedication.
- The PBPK model suggested that erdafitinib is an inhibitor towards intestinal P-gp. However, the model could not quantitatively predict the magnitude of the effect due to the uncertainty in translating in vitro Ki to in vivo Ki.
- The PBPK models could not quantitatively predict the effects of erdafitinib on a sensitive CYP3A substrate (such as midazolam) because the link between in vitro and in vivo was not appropriately established.
- The PBPK models are not adequate to predict the effects of erdafitinib on metformin (an OCT2 substrate) because the model under-predicted the effects of cimetidine and pyrimethamine on metformin at a dose level of 500 mg.
L1734: ## Background
L1735: Erdafitinib has high solubility and moderate to high permeability. The to-be-marketed formulations are 3mg, 4mg, and 5mg tablets. After single dose oral administration, median
L1736: Tmax was 2.5 hours (range: 2 to 6 hours) and oral absorption is near complete. Erdafitinib is highly bound to human plasma proteins due to high affinity binding to alpha-1-acid glycoprotein (AGP). The mean plasma unbound fractions (fups) of erdafitinib were 0.51% (CV 39.4%) and 0.32% (CV 64.0%), in healthy and cancer subjects, respectively (Table 10 of Clinical Pharmacology Summary). The blood-to-plasma (B:P) ratio was 0.60 across the concentration range of 15 to 750 ng/mL, with no concentration dependency. The mean effective half-life of erdafitinib in patients was 58.9 hours. Erdafitinib exposure (Cmax and AUC) increased in a dose-proportional manner across the dose range of 0.5 to 12 mg following single and multiple once daily administration. Multiple dose erdafitinib PK was time independent.
L1737: Erdafitinib is cleared via multiple pathways. Erdafitinib is primarily metabolized by cytochrome P450 (CYP) 2C9 and CYP3A4 to form the O-demethylated metabolite M6 and by CYP3A4 to form the N-dealkylated metabolite M8, a minor pathway. Unchanged erdafitinib was the major drug-related moiety in plasma; there were no major circulating metabolites. About 14.1% to 20.8% of total dose of erdafitinib was excreted in feces in the form of unchanged drug (68.7%) and M6 (approximately 24%). This portion of clearance was later assigned to biliary clearance as erdafitinib is almost completely absorbed and therefore the parent drug found in feces was due to the biliary excretion. About 18.7% of total dose of erdafitinib was excreted in urine with unchanged drug being the major entity.
L1738: In vitro, erdafitinib is a P-gp substrate, a time-dependent inhibitor (TDI) and inducer for CYP3A4, a P-gp and OCT2 inhibitor.
L1739: Since erdafitinib is a CYP2C9 substrate, CYP2C9 polymorphism is expected to have impact on the PK of erdafitinib in subjects with different CYP2C9 genotypes. Majority of subjects (healthy subjects and cancer patients) enrolled in erdafitinib PK studies carried CYP2C9 *1/*1, *1/*2 genotypes, or unknown. PK in two subjects with CYP2C9 *1/*3 genotype showed large variability with one having 23% increase and one having 45% decrease in clearance compared to the mean clearance in subjects with CYP2C9 *1/*1 genotype (Response to Clinical Pharmacology Information Request submitted on November 30, 2018). A parallel in vivo DDI study was conducted with fluconazole (400 mg QD), and itraconazole (200 mg QD). The study was conducted in healthy subjects with CYP2C9 *1/*1 and *1/*2 genotypes.
L1740: It was observed that Cmax and AUC0-24h were about 48% and 44% higher in cancer patients compared to healthy subjects, respectively, following a single dose administration. The observed mean (CV%) volumes of distribution (Vd/F) of erdafitinib were 28.8 L (51.2%) and 37.4 L (33.1%), respectively, in subjects with cancer and healthy subjects (Clinical Pharmacology Summary). The Applicant stated that the difference in PK was likely due to the differences in \alpha1-AGP levels and the plasma protein binding of erdafitinib in cancer patients and healthy subjects.
L1741: ## Methods
L1742: ## PBPK model structures and development
L1743: The erdafitinib PBPK model was developed based on in vitro and in vivo ADME data. Briefly, an Advanced Dissolution, Absorption and Metabolism (ADAM) model and a minimal PBPK model were utilized to describe the erdafitinib PK. The unbound fraction in plasma (fup) values were calculated based on erdafitinib binding to \alpha 1-AGP determined in vitro (Study FK10117) and the observed \alpha 1-AGP concentration in health subjects and cancer patients (Studies EDI1006 and EDI1001). The Applicant calculated a fup value of 0.0044 for healthy subjects and 0.0028 for cancer patients, which were used in the erdafitinib model. The volumes of distribution (Vss) were 0.42 L/kg, and 0.29 L/kg in healthy subjects and cancer patients, respectively. The Vss for healthy subjects was obtained from PK study EDI1003 and adjusted based on fup for cancer patients. Because erdafitinib is almost completely absorbed, the amount of parent drug found in the feces was considered due to the biliary excretion. The renal clearance and the biliary clearance were estimated based on the mass balance study (EDI1005). The in vivo intrinsic clearance (CLint) for each enzymatic pathway (CYP2C9, CYP3A4, and additional hepatic clearance) was derived from the in vitro phenotyping study, in vitro to in vivo scalars for CYP2C9 (tolbutamide) and CYP3A4 (disopyramide), and PK data in EDI1007. The estimated hepatic clearance consisted of CYP2C9 (39%), CYP3A4 (20%), additional hepatic clearance (8%).
L1744: To estimate the PK in subjects with various CYP2C9 genotypes, the Applicant incorporated different mean CYP2C9 abundance values in the erdafitinib model which are summarized in Table 141. The relative CYP2C9 activity to *1/*1 was estimated by comparing the mean enzyme abundance to the *1/*1 enzyme abundance. These values agree with the values in the Tables 5 and 6 in the Response to Clinical Pharmacology Information Request submitted on November 30, 2018.

L1753: A: Absorption, D: Distribution, E: Elimination, EGD: Electrochemical Gradient Driven; ADAM: Advanced Dissolution, Absorption and Metabolism; SD: single dose; MD: multiple dose; DDI: drug-drug interaction
L1754: It was noted that the Applicant made substantial changes to the Simcyp (V16) default compound files with justification. Specifically, changes were made to the midazolam, itraconazole and OH-itraconazole, metformin, and cimetidine files (see comparison in the Applicant's response to clinical pharmacology information request submitted on 30 November 2018).
L1755: The Applicant developed its own itraconazole and OH-itraconazole models which were different from the Simcyp library models in many parameters, such as logP, pKa, Fa, Ka, Vss, CYP3A4 clearance parameters (Vmax, Km, and fumic), CYP1A2 CLint (for itraconazole), CLr (for OH-itraconazole), and CYP3A4 Ki (for OH-itraconazole) (Response to Clinical Pharmacology Information Request submitted on November 30, 2018). The Applicant verified the itraconazole/OH-itraconazole models with itraconazole and OH-itraconazole plasma concentration-time profiles observed following multiple dose administration of itraconazole and a itraconazole/OH-itraconazole-midazolam (using the Applicant's midazolam model) DDI study.
L1756: In the fluconazole model, the Ki towards CYP2C9 was changed from 7.92 µM to 20.4 µM. The model was verified with CYP2C9 substrates, warfarin and tolbutamide. Of note, the revised Ki value has been verified by the software developer and updated in V17.
L1757: In the revised midazolam model, the Applicant reduced the fup value by 2-fold, developed an ADAM model for midazolam absorption, reduced the fugut value, and simplified the elimination by combining different nonlinear CYP3A4/5 pathways into linear CYP3A4 and additional clearance pathways. The revised midazolam model was verified by comparing the simulated against the observed mean plasma concentration time profiles for midazolam following a single oral administration of 7.5 mg from three publications.
L1758: ## PBPK model verification
L1759: The erdafitinib PBPK model predictions were compared to PK studies in healthy subjects and cancer patients (EDI1001, EDI1002, EDI1003, EDI1004, EDI1006), a mass balance study (EDI1005), and a DDI study (EDI1007) with fluconazole (a moderateCYP2C9 and CYP3A
L1760: inhibitor), and itraconazole (a strong CYP3A inhibitor) in subjects with CYP2C9 *1/*1 and *1/*2 genotypes. The in vivo studies for model verifications were summarized in Table 142.
L1761: ## PBPK model application
L1762: The developed PBPK models were used to simulate the following conditions.
L1763: - Erdafitinib PK in subjects with CYP2C9 *2/*2, *2/*3, and *3/*3 genotypes
- Effects of CYP2C9 and CYP3A4 inhibitors (fluconazole and itraconazole) and inducer (rifampin) on the PK of erdafitinib in subjects with various CYP2C9 genotypes
- Effects of erdafitinib on the PK of sensitive CYP3A4 substrates (midazolam, alprazolam, and triazolam)
- Effects of erdafitinib on the PK of a P-gp substrate (digoxin)
- Effects of erdafitinib on the PK of an OCT2 substrate (metformin)
L1764: ## Results
L1765: - Can the PBPK models provide a reasonable description of the PK of erdafitinib?
L1766: Yes, the PBPK models for erdafitinib described erdafitinib PK following a single dose of erdafitinib in healthy and cancer subjects (figures 1-3, and tables 1-3 in study report FK12149), and multiple dose of erdafitinib in cancer subjects (figures 2-3, and tables 2-3 in study report FK12149). The predicted versus the observed PK are shown in Figure 27 and Table 143.

L1774: Source: Tables 1-3 in study report FK12149, Tables 1-2 in Response to Clinical Pharmacology Information Request submitted on November 30, 2018, SD: single dose; MD: multiple dose; SS: steady state; AUCinf for SD, AUC72 for day 1 of MD, AUCtau for SS of MD.
L1775: The Applicant developed two populations, the healthy subjects and the cancer population. The difference between the two populations is the protein unbound fraction in plasma. It was observed that erdafitinib binds preferentially to \alpha 1-AGP and the unbound fraction was inversely related to \alpha 1-AGP concentration. The \alpha 1-AGP level was higher in cancer subjects than that in healthy subjects, which gave a lower unbound fraction in cancer subjects (mean 0.316% and CV 64.0%) than that in healthy subjects (mean 0.510% and CV 39.4%) (Table 10 in the Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Studies). The total exposure (Cmax and AUC24) to erdafitinib was 40% to 50% higher, and the free exposure (Cmax and AUC24) was about 20% higher in cancer subjects than those in healthy subjects (Table 2 in Response to Clinical Pharmacology Information Request submitted on November 30, 2018). The PBPK model incorporated fup values of 0.0028 and 0.0044, respectively, in cancer subjects and healthy subjects to describe the observed PK differences in the two populations. The PBPK models were able to describe the PK of erdafitinib in healthy and cancer subjects as shown in Figure 27. It appears that the model underestimated the observed variability in cancer subjects following 9 mg QD of erdafitinib (Figure 27 (C)). As it is unknown whether other physiology functions, such as renal, and liver functions which affect clearance and PK, might be different in the cancer subjects, the erdafitinib cancer PBPK model is considered as exploratory.
L1776: ## 2. Can the PBPK models be used to predict the PK of erdafitinib in various CYP2C9 genotype populations?
L1777: Yes. Erdafitinib is cleared by multiple pathways. The estimated contribution from each pathway varies depending on CYP2C9 genotype (see Table 144). The highest contribution from a specific pathway is about 40%.
L1778: **Table 144: Estimated contribution (%) of each clearance pathway to total clearance**

*2/*2	33	21	8	16	22
*2/*3	25	24	9	16	25
*3/*3	8	29	11	22	30
L1780: Relative CYP2C9 enzyme abundance was incorporated into the model in order to simulate erdafitinib PK in various CYP2C9 genotype populations. The relative enzyme abundance values were back-calculated from published studies on tolbutamide (a CYP2C9 probe
L1781: substrate). There have been literature reports on successful prediction of clearance for CYP2C9 substrates (Kusama M et al. Pharm Res. 2009 Apr;26(4):822-35) considering enzymatic activity, expression levels, and the contribution of CYP2C9 to total clearance. Table 145 is a summary of the CYP2C9 mediated clearance in various genotypes relative to *1/*1 for several CYP2C9 substrates, and erdafitinib. Large variability was observed with respect to relative clearance in various CYP2C9 genotypes. The designated relative clearance via CYP2C9 for erdafitinib was within the range observed for several CYP2C9 substrates.

L1790: *Predicted ratios are expressed as the fold changes compared to the corresponding parameter in CYP2C9 *1/*1
L1791: There are two uncertainties in the model predicting erdafitinib PK in subjects with various CYP2C9 genotypes: (1) the effects of erdafitinib on itself as a CYP3A4 perpetrator and substrate, and (2) the effects of erdafitinib on itself as a P-gp perpetrator and substrate).
L1792: In general, the confidence in predicting the time-dependent inhibition and inductions effects of erdafitinib on a CYP3A substrate is low (see discussion in the following section). The effects of erdafitinib on itself as a CYP3A4 perpetrator are expected to be low considering that the contribution of the CYP3A4 pathway to erdafitinib total clearance was about 20% in CYP2C9 extensive metabolizers, and linear and time-independent PK was observed. The reviewer explored the effects of erdafitinib as a CYP3A perpetrator by turning on and off the CYP3A4 interaction in the erdafitinib model and the results are shown in Table 146. The difference in PK was generally less than 10% between with and without the CYP3A4 interaction comparing the corresponding genotype groups. In addition, the reviewer conducted simulations to estimate erdafitinib PK in subjects without CYP29C activity. Overall, the simulations suggested that steady state AUC could be increased by 60% in CYP2C9 poor metabolizers compared to extensive metabolizers.
L1793: The effect of erdafitinib on itself as a P-gp inhibitor is expected to be low. Erdafitinib has high solubility and high permeability. The intestinal P-gp transport is likely to be saturated (above 0.3 \muM, i.e. 0.134 \mug/mL) following 9 mg dose of erdafitinib (36 \mug/mL estimated by 9 mg/250mL). Less than 20% of total dose was eliminated via renal clearance, suggesting that involvement of renal P-gp is not significant. The Applicant stated that the inhibitory effects of erdafitinib on renal P-gp is expected to be low, given that the value of I_{max,u}/IC_{50} was 0.0172 and less than basic cut-off value of 0.1 (Table 4 in Pharmacokinetics Written Summary). Because the erdafitinib exposure is linear across the dose range of 0.5 to 12 mg and time-independent, and itraconazole (a CYP3A and P-gp inhibitor) only increased erdafitinib AUC by 33% and did not affect Cmax, the effect of erdafitinib on itself as a P-gp inhibitor is expected to be low.
L1794: Based on the above assessment and considering that erdafitinib is not a substrate of major liver uptake transporters, the Applicant's approach is acceptable using PBPK analyses to predict erdafitinib PK in subjects with CYP2C9 *2/*2, *2/*3, and *3/*3 genotypes. The model predicted that erdafitinib exposure was about 60% higher in CYP2C9 poor metabolizers compared to that in CYP2C9 extensive metabolizers.
L1795: ## 3. Can the PBPK models be used to estimate the effects of CYP2C9/CYP3A modulators on erdafitinib PK?
L1796: Yes. The effects of CYP2C9/CYP3A modulators were verified with in vivo DDI study (EDI1007) between erdafitinib and itraconazole (a CYP3A and P-gp inhibitor) or fluconazole (a CYP2C9 and CYP3A inhibitor). The simulated geometric mean PK parameters (Cmax, AUC, CmaxR, and AUCR) were within 20% difference of the observed values (Table 147). The model slightly under predicted the effects of itraconazole on erdafitinib (1.18 vs. 1.34). Of note, the in vivo DDI study was a parallel design due to the long half-life of erdafitinib while the simulation was conducted using a cross-over design. The Applicant simulated multiple trials and compared erdafitinib PK in the absence and presence of itraconazole cross different trials, and an AUCR of 1.32 was obtained, which was close to the observed AUCR of 1.34.

L1800: The reviewer repeated the DDI simulations using the Applicant submitted workspace files and obtained similar results with numerical differences. The reviewer also conducted DDI simulation using the Simcyp (V17) default itraconazole/OH-itraconazole, and fluconazole models. The predicted DDI results were similar to the that obtained by the Applicant's models with numerical differences. The simulated itraconazole and OH-itraconazole PK profiles following 200 mg QD were similar using the Applicant's model vs. the Simcyp V17 default model for the first 10 days. The predicted plasma concentrations were higher using the Applicant's model than using the Simcyp V17 model after about 10 days of administration. The difference did not appear affecting the prediction of itraconazole's effect on erdafitinib PK probably because of the low contribution of the CYP3A pathway to the overall erdafitinib clearance.
L1801: Predicted erdafitinib exposure changes at steady state in the presence or absence of CYP2C9 or CYP3A4 modulators are summarized in Table 148. In each simulation, both the perpetrator and substrate were given from day 1 to day 22. The perpetrators were given either once daily (QD) or twice daily (BID). The erdafitinib exposure rations were expressed as fold changes relative to the exposure in subjects with CYP2C9 *1/*1 without comedication. A dual CYP2C9 and CYP3A4 inhibitor (such as fluconazole) may increase the erdafitinib exposure by approximately 60% to 100%, and a dual CYP2C9 and CYP3A inducer may decrease the erdafitinib exposure by
L1802: approximately 30%-60% compared to the exposure in subjects with CYP2C9 *1/*1 without comedication (Table 148). The erdafitinib CYP3A interaction effects on the DDI predictions are expected to be low due to the little contribution of CYP3A to erdafitinib clearance. Shutting down the erdafitinib CYP3A interaction in the model, the estimated AUC ratios were increased by 3% or 6% in CYP2C9 *1/*1 and *1/*3 subjects, respectively, with fluconazole or itraconazole, and no change with rifampin.

L1806: ## 4. Does the Applicant's PBPK analysis quantitatively predict the effects of erdafitinib on a sensitive CYP3A4 substrate?
L1807: No. In vitro, erdafitinib showed time-dependent inhibitory and induction potential towards CYP3A4. The inhibitory effect and the induction effect were integrated into the model separately. To quantitatively predict the in vivo inhibitory effect of erdafitinib from the in vitro system, the Applicant used erythromycin, the in vitro positive control compound, as the bridge. Specifically, the Applicant modified the Simcyp erythromycin model by using the in vitro determined inhibition parameters (CYP3A4 K_{app} = 2.26 vs. 23.2 \muM in Simcyp, K_{inact} = 1.72 vs. 2.25 1/h in Simcyp) and verified its effects on the PK of alprazolam (a sensitive CYP3A substrate). In response to a Clinical Pharmacology Information Request, the Applicant further
L1808: verified erythromycin model using midazolam and triazolam as substrates. The Although the modified erythromycin could predict its effects on the PK of alprazolam (predicted AUCR 3.15 vs. observed AUCR 2.47), the model over-predicted its effects on the PK of midazolam (predicted AUCR 13.8 vs. observed AUCR 4.41) and triazolam (predicted AUCR 12 vs. observed AUCR 3.65) by approximately 3-fold. These results demonstrated the uncertainties in predicting TDI effects of erythromycin directly using the in vitro parameters. Similarly, the uncertainties in predicting TDI effects of erdafitinib from in vitro parameters is expected. Additional analysis and experiences are needed to scale the in vitro TDI parameters for in vivo quantitative predictions indirectly from other perpetrator compounds.
L1809: The Ind_{slope} (slope of the fold induction vs. concentration plot) for erdafitinib model was obtained from the in vitro induction study and calibrated against the rifampin CYP3A induction slope value derived from Simcyp V16 and E_{max}/EC_{50} of rifampin from the same donor. A sensitivity analysis on the Ind_{slope}, in the range of 50-fold lower to 50-fold higher compared to the default value, suggested that erdafitinib does not have much induction effect on a sensitive CYP3A substrate.
L1810: Due to the uncertainties in predicting TDI effects of erdafitinib from in vitro parameters, the Applicant's PBPK analysis was not adequate to quantitatively predict the effects of erdafitinib on a sensitive CYP3A4 substrate.
L1811: 5. Can the PBPK model be used to qualitatively support the effects of erdafitinib on a P-gp substrate?
L1812: Yes. The Applicant used digoxin compound file provided in the Simcyp library as a P-gp substrate. Although the digoxin model did not incorporate P-gp in the kidney, it is not expected to affect the prediction of erdafitinib effects on a P-gp substrate (see discussion under Results #2). The IC_{50} values for P-gp obtained from the in vitro study were 0.67 \muM (based on extracellular concentration in medium) or 0.026 \muM (unbound intracellular concentration), respectively. A K_i of 0.026 \muM was used in the erdafitinib model to P-gp inhibition. Our in-house data suggested that in vitro K_i values generally under-predicted observed in vivo DDI between a P-gp perpetrator and digoxin. Sensitivity analysis on K_i was performed and showed that the maximal digoxin C_{max} and AUC ratio could be 1.58 and 1.25, respectively, in the presence and absence of erdafitinib.
L1813: Because there is an uncertainty in translating in vitro K_i to in vivo K_i towards P-gp, the PBPK model prediction was considered qualitative but not quantitative. The model suggested that erdafitinib had inhibitory potential towards intestinal P-gp.
L1814: The erdafitinib absorption was modeled by the ADAM model which incorporated a mean gastric transit time of 0.4 hour and a mean small intestinal transit time of 3.3 hours. Based on the erdafitinib absorption model simulation, majority of erdafitinib has moved out of small intestine after 6 hours of administration. After 6 hours of erdafitinib oral administration, the
L1815: simulated luminal and enterocyte concentrations were lower than the IC_{50} values based on extracellular concentration and unbound intracellular concentration, respectively (Figures 8 and 9 in study report FK12149). Therefore, staggered dosing between erdafitinib and a P-gp substrate may mitigate the risk of erdafitinib effects on a P-gp substrate.
L1816: ## 6. Can the PBPK models be used to estimate the effects of erdafitinib on an OCT2 substrate?
L1817: No. The Applicant indicated that the effects of cimetidine (an OCT2 and MATE inhibitor) and pyrimethamine (a MATE inhibitor) on metformin were verified for a dose of 250 mg metformin. The model under-predicted the interactions with cimetidine and pyrimethamine at a dose of 500 mg metformin. The reviewer tested the metformin model by completely blocking the OCT2 pathway, the MATEs pathway, and both OCT2 and MATEs pathways. The simulation suggested that the maximum AUCR (with vs. without an inhibitor) of metformin could be 2.16 at 250 mg dose level, and 1.76 at 500 mg dose level. A search in the University of Washington Drug Interaction Database Program suggested that dolutegravir (an OCT2 and MATEs inhibitor) could increase metformin AUC by 79% to 145% at metformin 500 mg dose level. Pyrimethamine could increase metformin AUC by 170% at metformin 500 mg dose level. The above analyses suggested that the metformin model could not capture the full spectrum of known DDIs. Therefore, the PBPK models could not be used to estimate the effects of erdafitinib on an OCT2 substrate.
L1818: ## Conclusions
L1819: In summary, the erdafitinib PBPK model is adequate to predict the PK in CYP2C9 poor metabolizers. The model predicted that erdafitinib exposure (AUC) could be increased approximately by 60% in subjects with CYP2C9 *3/*3 genotype compared to that in subjects with CYP2C9 *1/*1 genotype. The PBPK models are adequate to predict the effects of fluconazole (a moderate CYP2C9 and CYP3A inhibitor), itraconazole (a strong CYP3A inhibitor), and rifampin (a strong CYP3A and moderate CYP2C9 inducer) on erdafitinib PK. The model predicted that a dual CYP2C9 and CYP3A4 inhibitor (such as fluconazole) may increase the erdafitinib exposure by approximately 60% to 100%, and a dual CYP2C9 and CYP3A inducer may decrease the erdafitinib exposure by approximately 30%-60% compared to the exposure in subjects with CYP2C9 *1/*1 without comedication. The simulation suggested that erdafitinib had inhibitory potential towards intestinal P-gp. However, the PBPK analysis could not provide a quantitative estimation due to the uncertainty in translating *in vitro* K_i to *in vivo* K_i.
L1820: The PBPK models are not adequate to quantitatively predict the effects of erdafitinib on a sensitive CYP3A substrate (such as midazolam) because the link between *in vitro* and *in vivo* was not appropriately established. The PBPK models are not adequate to predict the effects of erdafitinib on metformin (an OCT2 substrate) because the model under-predicted the effects of cimetidine and pyrimethamine on metformin at a dose level of 500 mg.

L1864: Figure showing six plots related to population PKPD analysis of erdafitinib (BALVERSA™).
The plots are arranged in a 3x2 grid:
Top Left: Serum Phosphate concentrations (mg/dL) vs Population predictions (mg/dL, in scale). Shows a strong positive correlation, with data points clustered around the 1:1 line.
Top Right: Serum Phosphate concentrations (mg/dL) vs Individual predictions (mg/dL, in scale). Shows a strong positive correlation, with data points clustered around the 1:1 line.
Middle Left: Conditional weighted residuals vs Population predictions (mg/dL, in scale). Residuals are scattered around zero, indicating good model fit. Data points are categorized by study (1001, 2001, 10012).
Middle Right: Normalized prediction distribution errors vs Population predictions (mg/dL, in scale). Errors are scattered around zero, indicating good model fit. Data points are categorized by study (1001, 2001, 10012).
Bottom Left: Conditional weighted residuals vs Time since first dose (h). Residuals are scattered around zero, showing no clear trend over time. Data points are categorized by study (1001, 2001, 10012).
Bottom Right: Normalized prediction distribution errors vs Time since first dose (h). Errors are scattered around zero, showing no clear trend over time. Data points are categorized by study (1001, 2001, 10012).
Legend for all plots: Study 1001 (x), 2001 (diamond), 10012 (circle). Lines indicate 99% CI (dashed) and 95% CI (dotted).
L1865: Figure 14: pcVPC of the Final PK-PD Model for Serum Phosphate Concentrations Versus Time. The plot shows Serum Phosphate concentrations (mg/dL) on the Y-axis (ranging from 2 to 10) versus Time (h) on the X-axis (ranging from 0 to 15000). Observed data points are scattered, showing a peak around 4-5 hours and then a decline towards a steady state. The simulated data (red lines) includes the median (solid line), 5th and 95th percentiles (dashed lines), and the 95% confidence interval (blue and red shaded area). The observed data (black lines) also includes the median (solid line), 5th and 95th percentiles (dashed lines). The simulated and observed data generally align, showing a steady state concentration around 5.5 mg/dL.

L1868: Figure 17: Phosphate Concentrations vs Time for the Proposed PD Guided Dosing Regimen. The graph plots Total Serum PO4 (mg/dL) on the Y-axis (ranging from 2 to 10) against Time (days) on the X-axis (ranging from 0 to 133). The solid black line represents the median serum phosphate concentration, which starts around 3.2 mg/dL, peaks around 7.8 mg/dL around day 14, and then generally fluctuates between 5.0 and 7.0 mg/dL. The shaded areas represent the 50% and 90% prediction intervals (PIs). The 50% PI (lighter gray) and 90% PI (darker gray) are shown. The graph illustrates that the median concentration frequently exceeds 7 mg/dL, indicating that a significant proportion of subjects fall outside the target range.

L1878: Figure showing three panels (A, B, C) illustrating predicted and observed PK profiles of erdafitinib.
